## [Mining Design Principles from 28,568 Multi-Agent Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31709) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a two-phase pipeline that repurposes AFlow's MCTS optimizer to generate a corpus of 28,568 executable workflows across six composable operators and 26 Enigmata tasks, then mines design principles using a sandboxed AutoResearch loop with an L0-penalized validation objective. Across 300 feature-mining trials, it identifies effects that persist on held-out searches, such as Star-first workflows performing worse (Δ = -0.045) and SharedMemory outperforming debate (Δ = +0.135). This work moves multi-agent system design beyond trial-and-error and folk wisdom by turning discarded search trajectories into a statistical testbed for discovering and validating reproducible design principles. It could provide empirical guidance for researchers and practitioners building LLM-based multi-agent workflows, reducing reliance on manual, small-scale studies. The pipeline retains all intermediate rounds from AFlow's MCTS optimizer to build the workflow corpus, and the AutoResearch loop mines orchestration code and prompt templates under an L0-penalized validation objective. The identified effects include prompt specificity and placeholder-based context routing yielding gains comparable to communication-structure selection (Δ = +0.118).

## [Compact-Bench Quantifies Context Compaction Damage in Long-Horizon Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31710) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Compact-Bench, a benchmark that pairs full-history and summary-only continuations from a shared repository state to isolate the effect of context compaction. Across 35 SWE-bench repairs, 146 agentic sessions, and 14 summarizer backbones, it finds stable damage ranging from 5% to 64% of 22 full-history successes, with Claude Opus 4.8 preserving the most repairs and DeepSeek-Flash damaging the most. Context compaction is a standard technique for letting coding agents work beyond their context windows, but this study shows it can silently erase task-critical state. The findings suggest that retaining relevant information alone does not guarantee successful task continuation, which has direct implications for how agent systems are designed and evaluated. LLM summaries retain at most 16.0% of recurring details, compared with 79.1% for mechanical extraction, and production summaries retain only 15.7% at the first boundary. Failures recur across methods: one repair is damaged by 13 of 14 backbones, and another by all 5 prompts, while with Kimi-K3 as summarizer the Kimi Code prompt preserves the most task continuations (19/22) and Claude Code retains the most recurring details (15.0%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon coding agents often exceed their LLM context windows, so systems use context compaction—replacing older history with a summary—to keep working. SWE-bench is a widely used benchmark where models must resolve real GitHub issues in repositories, making it a realistic testbed for such agents. A summarizer backbone is the underlying LLM that generates the compacted summary, and different backbones and prompts can behave very differently.

It also cautions that LLM annotators agree much more closely with each other than with the human reviewer's topic assignments, raising concerns for automated annotation.

## [Differentiable Diffusion Conditioning Enables End-to-End Physics-Informed Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31721) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Differentiable Diffusion Conditioning (D2C), an end-to-end gradient-based optimizer for offline model-based optimization that turns a frozen conditional diffusion model into a test-time optimizer by backpropagating gradients from surrogate objectives and physics-informed penalties through a deterministic reverse denoising chain. It achieves the strongest results on two real-world tasks—laser pulse optimization for inertial confinement fusion and sustainable data-center workload scheduling—and the best average rank across four Design-Bench tasks. This work bridges generative modeling and physics-informed optimization, offering a way to refine designs against precise physical objectives without test-time access to the true objective, which could impact expensive engineering design problems in fusion energy and sustainable computing. It also addresses key limitations of existing offline MBO methods, such as conservatism and unconstrained surrogate exploitation. D2C leverages deterministic sampling to make the reverse denoising chain differentiable with respect to the conditioning signal, and it is implemented in a composed proposer-evaluator framework. The method optimizes conditioning variables at test time using gradients from frozen surrogate objectives and physics-informed penalties, avoiding retraining of the diffusion model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Offline model-based optimization (MBO) aims to find optimal designs using only a fixed dataset of designs and scores, without querying the true objective. Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise data, and conditional diffusion models can generate samples guided by additional information. Deterministic sampling in diffusion models follows a fixed path, making the generation process reproducible and differentiable, which is key to D2C's approach.

## [Causal Bounds and Post-Training Recipes for Chain-of-Thought Monitorability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper establishes that the minimal-criterion sensitivity metric (TPR_mc), widely used to evaluate chain-of-thought monitorability, is an upper bound on the causal detection rate, and derives a complementary lower bound for tighter evaluation. It then proposes three intervention-aware post-training methods—off-policy self-distillation, reinforcement learning, and on-policy self-distillation—that generally improve CoT monitorability across model-monitor configurations. This work shows that a popular CoT monitorability metric can overestimate how often monitors detect behaviors actually caused by interventions, which matters for AI safety evaluations that rely on CoT monitoring to catch harmful or deceptive reasoning. The proposed training recipes suggest that models can be trained to make their reasoning more transparent, potentially strengthening oversight of LLM reasoning. The authors construct a training dataset of paired control-intervention tasks spanning multiple intervention types and train separate model variants for each method; all three methods generally improve monitorability over base models under the extended evaluation framework. The code is available at https://github.com/xinyue-lxy/learning-to-be-monitored, though the work is a preprint with limited community discussion so far.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring refers to inspecting a reasoning model's intermediate natural-language reasoning steps to detect latent misbehavior or misaligned intentions that the final output might hide. Intervention-based evaluations test whether a monitor can detect the role of an intervention from the CoT when a target behavior occurs, often using minimal-criterion sensitivity (TPR_mc). This paper argues that observing a behavior under intervention does not imply causation, and provides a causal framework to evaluate and improve CoT monitorability.

## [Context Compaction Threshold Has No Effect on 1M-Context Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31778) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.31778) evaluates five context compaction strategies within the OpenCode agent scaffold across three frontier models and three long-horizon benchmarks (PaperBench, ProgramBench, PostTrainBench). It finds that for the two 1M-context models, the compaction threshold has no measurable effect on task performance, while GPT-5.3-Codex, the model with the smallest context window, performs worse at lower thresholds on PaperBench. This is the first systematic study of context compaction with frontier models on realistic long-horizon agentic tasks, filling a gap left by prior work on smaller open-weight models and synthetic trajectories. The findings suggest current benchmarks are 'compaction-friendly' and that compaction can silently drop critical task constraints, which has direct implications for the reliability of coding agents. No compaction method outperformed default self-summarization, and methods that discard more content cost some models up to 20 points while leaving others unchanged. On ProgramBench, the first compaction summary already drops about one third of the task's anti-cheating rules on average, and on real SWE-chat sessions, instructions about how the agent should work are dropped around 6 times more often than specifications of what to build.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agentic tasks often exceed an LLM's context window, so agent scaffolds use context compaction—summarizing or discarding older context—to continue working. Compaction is inherently lossy and can cause agents to forget task constraints, instructions, or earlier reasoning. OpenCode is an open-source coding agent scaffold, and PaperBench, ProgramBench, and PostTrainBench are benchmarks designed to test agents on extended, multi-step tasks.

## [PrefO2O Enables Zero-Shot Preference-Based RL via Offline-to-Online Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31812) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PrefO2O introduces an offline-to-online latent preference transfer framework that learns a task-invariant latent preference space from a small amount of offline preference data on source tasks, enabling zero-shot preference-based reinforcement learning on unseen target tasks without any human feedback. The method uses confidence-weighted optimal transport alignment to generate reliable pseudo-preference supervision for online reward adaptation. This work addresses a key scalability bottleneck in preference-based RL—the need for expensive human preference labels for every new task—by transferring preference structures across tasks, which could significantly reduce human effort in robot learning and RL alignment. It demonstrates that zero-shot PbRL is feasible and can match or exceed methods trained with real human feedback, with generalization across different robot embodiments. The core idea is to disentangle transferable preference structures from task-specific variations in a learned latent manifold, allowing transfer under distribution shift. Experiments on robotic manipulation and locomotion benchmarks show PrefO2O substantially outperforms existing preference-transfer baselines and generalizes across multi-to-one and one-to-one transfer settings as well as different robot embodiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL) trains agents using human comparisons between trajectory pairs instead of hand-crafted reward functions, making it promising for aligning robot behaviors with human intent. However, PbRL typically requires collecting new human preference labels for each task, which is expensive and limits scalability. Offline-to-online transfer aims to leverage offline data to improve online learning, and optimal transport provides a way to align distributions, which PrefO2O uses to generate pseudo-preference labels.

## [SEVI: Evidential Safety Critic for Safe Real-World Robot RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31829) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SEVI introduces an Evidential Safety Critic that predicts both future constraint-violation risk and epistemic uncertainty in a single forward pass, and overrides unsafe task-policy actions with an offline-trained recovery policy during online robot learning. It was evaluated on two simulated manipulation tasks and a real-world USB insertion task, reducing unsafe events by 13.4 to 57.1 percent relative to recovery-based safe RL while achieving the highest final task success. Unsafe interactions during online learning are a major barrier to deploying real-world reinforcement learning on physical robots, so a method that filters risky actions with low latency while preserving task performance could make contact-rich manipulation training safer and more practical. The approach is relevant to researchers and practitioners working on safe RL, robot learning, and uncertainty-aware decision making. SEVI evaluates each task-policy action before execution and triggers the offline recovery policy when the action is predicted unsafe or when the prediction lacks sufficient evidential support, and it maintains low-latency execution-time filtering. The evaluation covers in-distribution and distribution-shifted conditions, though the real-world validation is limited to a single USB insertion task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Safe reinforcement learning typically adds a safety critic that estimates the expected cumulative cost of actions, allowing the policy to be penalized for constraint violations. Evidential deep learning estimates epistemic uncertainty by having a model output evidence for its predictions in a single pass, which is useful for detecting when the model is operating outside its knowledge. SEVI combines these ideas so that a robot can refuse or override actions that are either predicted unsafe or insufficiently supported by evidence, using a recovery policy trained offline to move toward lower-risk states.

## [PRISM: Self-Adjusting Multi-View Fusion for Urban Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31850) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PRISM introduces a self-adjusting multi-view fusion framework that uses sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routing to learn region-specific view allocations and a PID-inspired regulation mechanism to mitigate gradient conflicts. Experiments on three large-scale urban datasets show competitive performance against strong multi-view baselines with more stable optimization and balanced region representations. The work addresses two structural issues in urban representation learning—region-varying modality importance and persistent gradient imbalance—that static fusion and joint optimization overlook, potentially improving downstream tasks like check-in prediction, crime forecasting, and service demand estimation. It offers a principled approach for researchers in urban computing and multi-modal learning dealing with heterogeneous spatial data. PRISM adapts sparse MoE routing so that different regions can weight modalities differently, and its PID-inspired regulation mechanism targets gradient conflicts during multi-objective training. The abstract reports competitive results on three large-scale urban datasets, though specific performance gains and limitations are not detailed in the summary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Urban region representation learning encodes heterogeneous signals such as mobility flows, points of interest, and land-use patterns into unified embeddings for downstream prediction tasks. Multi-view fusion combines these different data views, but most existing methods use globally shared fusion weights and joint optimization, implicitly assuming that all modalities are equally important everywhere and that training objectives are naturally balanced. Sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a technique where a routing network selects a small subset of expert sub-networks for each input, enabling input-specific computation; PID control is a feedback mechanism that adjusts based on proportional, integral, and derivative terms to reduce error over time.

SWE-Clar introduces a 100-task benchmark built from real high-value Upwork commissions (each with at least US$4,000 in total payments) to evaluate whether coding agents can clarify incomplete client requirements and then implement greenfield software. Each task involves multi-round interaction between a coding agent and a client agent, followed by isolated runs for code implementation and clarification assessment using 512 questions with reference answers. This benchmark targets a real and underexplored problem: requirement clarification for coding agents on greenfield freelance tasks, which is critical for autonomous software delivery. It contributes a new evaluation paradigm (clarification before implementation, explicit understanding tests) that could influence how agentic coding systems are designed and assessed. The benchmark isolates clarification assessment from code implementation to prevent assessment questions from guiding implementation, and experiments with nine frontier models show that even GPT-6 Astra, the highest observed implementation scorer, achieves only a 67% task pass rate. The dataset and evaluation code are available at https://github.com/upwork/SWE-Clar.

## [Curriculum Learning's Effectiveness Depends on Reward Sparsity and Competing Optima](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31884) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper constructs a minimal model of policy learning to analyze why curriculum learning helps more in reinforcement learning than in supervised learning. It shows that curricula are essential for sparse rewards but only marginally beneficial for dense rewards, especially when the agent has conflicting goals of minimizing effort and maximizing reward. This work provides a rigorous theoretical explanation for the varying effectiveness of curriculum learning, helping practitioners decide when to invest in curriculum design. It also highlights the role of competing optima in reward landscapes, which could inform better reward shaping and training strategies in reinforcement learning. The study uses an open-loop, episodic target interception task and analyzes REINFORCE-based policy learning both mathematically and numerically. It finds that curricula address two issues: making rewards less sparse to speed up learning and shaping the reward landscape to steer agents away from bad local optima.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Curriculum learning is a training strategy where a model learns from easier examples or tasks first and gradually progresses to harder ones, inspired by how humans and animals learn. In reinforcement learning, an agent learns by interacting with an environment and receiving rewards, but when rewards are sparse (rarely given), learning becomes difficult. REINFORCE is a basic policy gradient algorithm that updates the policy directly based on received rewards.

## [ImpasseBench Exposes Coding Agents' Evidence-Action Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31895) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces ImpasseBench, a benchmark that injects twelve realistic environment blockers into otherwise solvable repair tasks and marks the step at which collected evidence becomes sufficient to justify stopping. Across eight frontier models and 19,371 trajectories, nearly every run that needed outside help collected sufficient evidence, yet a third still exhausted their budget, and all detected unsafe requests occurred after that evidence point. This work identifies a concrete reliability failure mode—the evidence-action gap—in coding agents and shows that explicit escalation tools or external monitors can substantially mitigate it, which matters for anyone deploying autonomous coding agents in production. It also highlights that a fired escalation is not yet a useful handoff, pointing to needed improvements in agent reporting. An escalation tool cuts budget exhaustion from 34.0% to 6.7%, and an external monitor for models that ignore the tool nearly triples task success after environment restoration. However, escalation tickets reliably name the blocker but often omit the agent's own progress and verification status, limiting their usefulness as handoffs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents are LLM-based systems trained to complete software tasks autonomously, and they tend to keep trying even when the environment makes completion impossible. The paper defines the evidence-action gap as the disconnect between collecting sufficient evidence that outside help is needed and actually acting on it, and ImpasseBench measures this by scoring agent actions relative to the step where evidence becomes sufficient.

Researchers introduced S-SALAAD, a unified framework that jointly learns model weights along with their low-rank and structured sparse components during training, enabling a single checkpoint to be compressed to multiple memory budgets. Experiments on Llama2 and Qwen3 models, covering both pretraining and distillation, show that S-SALAAD advances the empirical Pareto frontier in perplexity, memory, and inference latency across multiple hardware platforms. This work addresses a critical deployment barrier: reducing parameters does not automatically speed up inference, especially with unstructured sparsity that incurs indexing overhead and irregular memory access. By learning hardware-compatible structured sparsity, S-SALAAD translates elastic compressibility into actual lower memory usage and latency, making local inference of large models on edge devices more practical. The framework supports a family of hardware-compatible structured sparsity patterns and couples structure learning with budget-aware compression, along with dedicated CPU and GPU kernels that exploit the learned structure for acceleration. The approach is evaluated on Llama2 and Qwen3 models, but as an arXiv preprint it has not yet undergone peer review.

## [ENCL: Efficient Neuromorphic Continual Learning via Local Learning and Sub-Network Freezing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31927) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ENCL introduces a lightweight local learning rule and a sub-network freezing strategy to enable efficient neuromorphic continual learning without data replay or backpropagation-based surrogate gradients. On task-incremental learning benchmarks (Split MNIST, Fashion MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100), it achieves accuracy comparable to state-of-the-art replay methods while reducing memory cost by up to 88%, training time by up to 51%, average power by up to 40%, and energy by up to 65%. This work addresses two major bottlenecks in neuromorphic continual learning—memory-hungry experience replay and costly global surrogate-gradient training—making on-device continual learning more practical for energy-constrained edge and neuromorphic hardware. It could enable deployed systems to adapt to new data at runtime with significantly lower memory, time, and energy overhead. ENCL uses a temporally and spatially local learning rule for credit assignment and freezes task-specific sub-networks to avoid architecture expansion, training each task with few epochs and adjusted learning rate and timestep. Results are reported for task-incremental learning scenarios, and the approach is compared against state-of-the-art data/experience replay methods.

## [Spectra Uses Implicit Neural Reconstruction Dynamics for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Spectra is a new deepfake detection framework that fits an implicit neural representation (INR) to each image and analyzes its internal activations to extract multi-level reconstruction heatmaps, which are then fused bidirectionally with semantic features. Experiments on multiple benchmarks show improved robustness and cross-dataset generalization over detectors relying solely on RGB appearance or semantic representations. Existing deepfake detectors often overfit to generator-specific cues and degrade on unseen manipulation techniques or compression levels, so a representation that captures how an image is reconstructed rather than how it appears could meaningfully improve generalization. This matters for media forensics and AI security, where detectors must keep pace with rapidly evolving synthesis models. The method extracts multi-level reconstruction heatmaps from image-specific INRs and jointly models them with semantic features via a bidirectional fusion mechanism, enabling direct interaction between image semantics and reconstruction artifacts. The authors also show the representation extends to face presentation attack detection, suggesting a unified framework for facial attack detection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit neural representations (INRs), also called neural fields, are networks that parameterize continuous signals such as images directly as the weights of a multilayer perceptron, mapping coordinates to values rather than working with discrete pixels. Deepfakes are AI-generated or manipulated media, and most detectors look for RGB appearance cues or frequency-domain artifacts left by synthesis. Because these cues are often generator-specific, detectors can fail when synthesis models evolve or images are recompressed.

Frontier closed-source models outperform open-weight models at every level, with the best closed model leading the best open-weight model by 16.3% on Perception, 22.4% on Reasoning, and 37.4% on Insight.

Features selected by density-adjusted correlations show a small positive shift of their target metric as a family, whereas features selected by raw correlations show none; an atlas provides MIDI, piano-roll, and audio examples for every feature of the targeted screen.

A new arXiv preprint (2610.32039) provides a theoretical framework showing that existing supervised contrastive learning objectives and their balanced variants do not form a regular simplex at the optimum under long-tailed distributions, because they remain dependent on the number of samples per class. The authors introduce structural balancing and propose a fully balanced contrastive objective that provably achieves a regular simplex geometry at its optimum, with theoretical and empirical results showing improved long-tailed recognition and transfer learning performance. This work clarifies a gap between the assumed and actual geometry of learned representations in long-tailed supervised contrastive learning, offering a principled fix that could lead to more balanced representation spaces and better performance on tasks where class frequencies are highly skewed. It is relevant to researchers and practitioners working on representation learning, long-tailed recognition, and transfer learning. The analysis shows that representative contrastive objectives fail to form a regular simplex at the optimum due to their dependence on per-class sample counts, and the proposed structural balancing is designed to remove this dependence. The method is evaluated on long-tailed recognition and transfer learning tasks, though as a preprint it has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Supervised contrastive learning (SupCon) is a technique that uses label information to pull representations of same-class samples together and push different-class samples apart, and it has been effective for learning visual representations. A regular simplex is a highly symmetric geometric configuration where class prototypes are equally spaced, often considered an ideal arrangement for classification. Long-tailed distributions, where a few classes have many samples and most have few, pose challenges for representation learning because models tend to bias toward frequent classes.

## [ConceptACP: Sparse Autoencoder-Based Concept Erasure for Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32059) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Concept Acupuncturist (ConceptACP), a machine unlearning framework that maps semantic concepts to cross-attention blocks in diffusion U-Nets and uses sparse autoencoders to construct targeted correction signals for precise concept removal. Instantiated on SDXL-Turbo, it improves forgetting success rate by 36.34 percentage points over the best baseline in one-step generation while preserving image quality and prompt fidelity. This work addresses the growing need for machine unlearning in generative models to remove harmful or policy-violating content, offering an efficient inference-free fine-tuning approach that could be integrated into model deployment pipelines. It also demonstrates how interpretability tools like sparse autoencoders can enable precise, localized edits in large diffusion models. ConceptACP localizes the most concept-relevant cross-attention block via interpretability-guided analysis, avoiding full diffusion trajectory optimization, and uses SAE-derived sparse feature activations to mitigate concept entanglement. The method eliminates the need for external inference-time modules and generalizes across other Stable Diffusion backbones, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising latent representations in a U-Net, where cross-attention blocks inject text conditioning. Machine unlearning aims to selectively remove concepts from a trained model without retraining from scratch, but concept representations are distributed and entangled, making precise edits difficult. Sparse autoencoders are neural networks that learn sparse, interpretable feature representations, often used to disentangle concepts in activation space.

## [EEG foundation models often no better than random initialization, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32067) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study evaluated five publicly released EEG foundation models and an in-house JEPA-style model under a unified protocol with three key controls: untrained encoders, spectral baselines, and validation-based layer selection. The results show that random initialization matches or beats pretraining for half of the models on sleep and emotion tasks, and no model outperforms a five-band log-power baseline on emotion or motor imagery. This challenges the claimed benefits of pretraining in EEG foundation models and highlights methodological flaws such as test-score-based layer selection, which inflates performance. The findings could lead to more rigorous evaluation standards and affect how researchers and practitioners interpret gains from large-scale pretraining in neuroscience. Picking the probed layer by test score raises the layer-wise peak by an average of +0.034 and reverses the peak-versus-output gap in 9 of 36 cells. After full fine-tuning, only 5 of 30 pairs gain beyond twice their seed spread, and one clear loss of -0.290 AUROC persists under matched learning rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: EEG foundation models are large-scale self-supervised models trained on diverse EEG data to learn universal representations, analogous to foundation models in language. JEPA (Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture) is a self-supervised learning approach proposed by Yann LeCun that predicts in latent space. Band-power baselines are simple spectral features (e.g., log-power in five frequency bands) commonly used in EEG analysis. This study questions whether the complex pretraining of EEG foundation models truly provides advantages over such simple baselines and random initialization.

## [AgentTrace fingerprints black-box LLM agents via execution traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentTrace is a new active fingerprinting framework that identifies the foundation model behind a black-box LLM agent by injecting structurally ambiguous probes to trigger tokenization-sensitive serialization failures at tool interfaces, then modeling the subsequent repair trajectories with lightweight Markov chains. It reports 96.0% attribution accuracy under adversarial identity spoofing, and notably distinguishes intra-family variants sharing identical tokenizers, such as Llama-3 8B and 70B. Verifying which model powers an autonomous agent is a real security and accountability problem, since semantic attribution can be easily obfuscated by system prompts and active watermarking conflicts with schema-constrained tool generation. A robust execution-level fingerprint gives auditors and platform operators a way to detect model substitution or spoofing in deployed agent systems. The approach relies on the observation that initial serialization failures give only coarse separation across model families, while the temporal sequence of repair operations reveals fine-grained, model-specific recovery behavior. Sequential repair modeling substantially outperforms unordered behavioral statistics and remains robust against prompt obfuscation and temperature scaling, though the abstract does not detail the probe set size or the range of models evaluated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM fingerprinting aims to determine, in a black-box setting, whether a target model matches a reference model, and it has become increasingly relevant as APIs and agent platforms may silently substitute models. Tokenization is the process by which language models split text into tokens, and different models—even within one family—can tokenize and serialize structured tool-call arguments differently. Markov chains are probabilistic models of state transitions, here used to characterize the sequence of repair steps an agent takes after a failed tool call.

## [REACT: Early Activation Steering Controls Chain-of-Thought Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REACT introduces a conditional activation intervention that learns a safety direction from paired safe and unsafe chain-of-thought trajectories and applies it once to an early hidden state, finding that the most effective control window is only 2-3 tokens into generation across three distilled reasoning models. On DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B, the intervention raises fully safe CoT-answer outputs on StrongREJECT-273 from 29.06% to 82.05% while keeping benign refusal and usefulness near baseline. This work separates risk readability from effective control, showing that the point where risk is easiest to detect is not necessarily where intervention is most effective, which has direct implications for designing safety mechanisms in reasoning models. It suggests that lightweight, single-shot interventions early in generation can substantially improve safety without retraining or modifying model weights. The method trains separate safety directions for each of the first five CoT positions and evaluates ungated edits with full post-edit regeneration, with model weights frozen and all subsequent tokens generated without further edits. The effect falls to approximately zero at the fourth and fifth tokens on the 8B model, even though later states can be more predictive of risk, and results are consistent across three generation seeds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a technique that modifies a language model's internal activations using learned steering vectors to reliably alter outputs without retraining, and it has been used to modulate properties such as sentiment, topic, and truthfulness. Chain-of-thought generation in reasoning models produces intermediate reasoning tokens before the final answer, offering an opportunity to intervene before the model completes its response. DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B is an 8-billion-parameter model distilled from DeepSeek-R1's reasoning traces on a Llama-3.1-8B base, and it is one of the distilled reasoning models studied in this work.

## [Tampered ML Benchmark Tests LLM Judgment of ML Research Methodology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Tampered ML, a benchmark of 1,858 item-pairs where each pair contains an experiment proposal from a published ML paper and a minimally edited counterfactual with a targeted methodological flaw. Models must identify the sounder proposal in both presentation orders, and even the frontier model Opus 5 achieves only 77.9% accuracy. This matters because automating AI alignment research requires systems that can reliably identify flaws in research proposals, and the finding that frontier models are far from reliable and highly order-sensitive highlights a key gap for AI alignment automation. It also provides an actionable benchmark for measuring progress in this capability. A human validation study on a stratified sample estimates that 86% of items are valid (95% CI: 81-91%), and models tend to favor flawed designs that appear more specific, transparent, or conventional. The benchmark requires evaluation in both presentation orders to detect order sensitivity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment research aims to ensure AI systems behave as intended, and automating parts of this research could accelerate progress. However, such automation requires LLMs that can judge the quality of research proposals, including spotting methodological flaws. Tampered ML is a benchmark designed to test this specific capability by pairing original proposals with subtly flawed counterfactuals.

## [ImageRSI: Verification-Driven Self-Improvement for Agentic Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32120) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ImageRSI introduces a framework that keeps the image generator frozen while using five-dimensional verification and repair, a cross-task experience library, and offline judge updates to improve complex text-to-image generation. It also releases ImageReq-Bench, a benchmark of 961 requests with evaluation checklists, and achieves Checklist Strict Accuracy (CSA) of 52.6% on MindBench and 65.66% on ImageReq-Bench. This work addresses the reliability gap in complex text-to-image generation by enabling systematic self-improvement without retraining the generator, which could lower costs and improve adaptability for agentic image systems. The new benchmark and verification-driven approach may also standardize evaluation and inspire similar recursive improvement frameworks in other generative tasks. Candidate experiences enter the library only after paired A/B validation, and the judge is updated with supervised fine-tuning and GRPO on accumulated image-judgment records, then evaluated before reuse. The framework is evaluated with Nano Banana Pro as the generator, and the paper compares system outcomes across different judge-training configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Complex text-to-image requests often require satisfying multiple constraints, and while verifying and repairing an individual output can fix that image, the repair knowledge may not transfer to other requests. ImageRSI tackles this by freezing the generator and improving the surrounding agentic system through verification, experience sharing, and judge updates. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that uses group-normalized advantage estimation without a value critic, and it is used here to train the judge.

## [LARE: Loss-Adaptive Reweighting Boosts LLM Pretraining on Data Mixtures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32141) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LARE (Loss-Adaptive Reweighting under fixed Exposure), a method that automatically adjusts domain weights based on running loss statistics during pretraining. In experiments with a 1.5B model trained on 240B tokens, LARE improved all five benchmarks by an average of +7.84 percentage points over a matched direct mixture, including +17.06 pp on GSM8K and +9.00 pp on MATH. This work reveals that loss reweighting provides an independent control beyond data-mixture ratios, offering a new way to mitigate catastrophic forgetting and improve multi-domain capabilities without additional data exposure. It could significantly impact LLM pretraining research by enabling more efficient joint training across knowledge, math, and code domains. LARE uses a normalized inverse-square-root rule motivated by gradient noise analysis to balance relative loss scales without changing the sampled mixture or requiring extra runs to search for weights. The method was evaluated across knowledge, mathematics, and code domains, showing consistent gains over stage-based training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are often pretrained in successive stages (e.g., general knowledge then specialized math/code), which can cause catastrophic forgetting of earlier knowledge. Data mixture ratios control what proportion of each domain is sampled, but they do not directly control how strongly each domain's loss contributes to learning. Loss reweighting dynamically adjusts the importance of each domain's loss during training, potentially allowing a single model to retain multiple capabilities without additional data exposure.

## [New LoRA Initialization Stabilizes Asymmetric Learning Rates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies initialization-induced noise as the cause of instability in LoRA+ asymmetric learning rates and proposes a new initialization scheme, called InitAA, that scales the variance of the down-projection matrix A as σ_A^2 ∝ n^{-2} while keeping B_0=0. This reduces initialization-noise energy by an additional factor of 1/n, stabilizing training and widening the stable learning-rate region. LoRA fine-tuning is highly sensitive to learning-rate choices, and LoRA+ introduces an additional hyperparameter (the ratio η_B/η_A) that can cause training collapse under standard initialization. This work provides a theoretical explanation and a simple fix that makes asymmetric learning rates more robust, potentially reducing tuning costs for practitioners fine-tuning large language models. Through a signal-noise decomposition of LoRA dynamics, the authors show that under standard initialization the update contains a noise term whose magnitude depends on η_B, the initialization scale β, and the rank r. Empirically, InitAA reduces best-ratio drift, prevents high-ratio collapse, and makes the width-based guideline η_B ≈ n η_A a useful starting point for finite pretrained models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes the pretrained model weights and injects trainable low-rank matrices A and B into each layer, drastically reducing the number of trainable parameters. LoRA+ is a variant that uses different learning rates for A and B, typically a larger rate for B, to improve adaptation. However, the ratio between these rates is an extra hyperparameter that can make training unstable, especially with standard initialization where A is randomly initialized and B is set to zero.

## [Sparsity regularization induces functional modularity in multitask networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32153) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper shows that sparsity-inducing regularization causes multitask neural networks to develop compact, task-selective subnetworks called 'functional tokens' without predefined module boundaries. Experiments on multilayer perceptrons and a lightweight transformer reveal that this specialization emerges preferentially in deeper layers during training. This work connects sparsity regularization to the emergence of modular computation, offering a new lens for interpretability and modular deep learning. It suggests that sparsity constraints can shape not only parameter efficiency but also the organization and reuse of learned functions, which could impact how multitask models are designed and analyzed. Selectively retaining functional tokens preserves their associated tasks while degrading others, and extracted tokens can be recombined through alternative pathways to reconstruct a composite computation without retraining. The strength and form of regularization control module compactness and specialization, while task weighting alters functional allocation.

Principia is a new LLM-based framework that constructs and continually refines scientific principles at test time by forming comparative causal hypotheses from related inputs and outcomes, then synthesizing recurring relationships into inductive principles. It outperforms baselines on benchmarks for molecular optimization, protein engineering, gene perturbation, and molecular property prediction, with code available on GitHub. This work addresses the bottleneck of translating observations into scientific principles, which typically requires substantial domain expertise and time, potentially accelerating AI-driven scientific discovery across multiple domains. It demonstrates that LLMs can autonomously evolve task-specific knowledge during test-time discovery, moving beyond static internal knowledge or external sources. The framework iteratively applies constructed hypotheses and principles to obtain new observations, revises hypotheses based on these observations, and reconstructs principles, enabling continuous knowledge evolution. A new arXiv paper (2610.32213) empirically shows that at a fixed model size, allocating parameters to fewer, wider attention heads improves long-context retrieval performance, while pretraining loss and short-context non-retrieval tasks remain largely unaffected. The study also finds that models with wider heads have a higher proportion of retrieval heads, and that the benefit persists even when attention projection weights are frozen during long-context continual pretraining. The ablation studies show that retrieval gains from wider heads occur even when attention projection weights are frozen during long-context continual pretraining, indicating that wider heads also enable more effective adaptation of embeddings and MLPs. ## [PFIR teaches 3D MLLMs part-level functional reasoning to disambiguate similar objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32262) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PFIR (Part-level Functional Inference for Recognition), a method that reverses the information flow in 3D MLLMs from function to identity, built on PartNeXt part segmentation and a new 507K QA dataset with functional chain-of-thought (geometry → function → identity). Using a progressive curriculum ending in dual-objective GRPO, PFIR raises zero-shot accuracy from 60.5% to 66.7% under a strict dual out-of-distribution protocol (Objaverse → ModelNet40; XYZRGB → XYZ-only), with gains concentrated 2.6× more on geometrically ambiguous samples. This work addresses a concrete failure mode of 3D MLLMs—pattern matching instead of functional reasoning—and shows that part-level functional inference can substantially improve generalization to geometrically similar objects, which is important for robotics, embodied AI, and 3D scene understanding. The release of a 507K QA dataset and code is likely to spur further research in 3D vision and reasoning. The method uses a per-sample Geometric Confusion Score (GCS) to expose the failure, and the two RL objectives (strict functional reasoning and descriptive grounding) yield nearly orthogonal task vectors (cos = 0.056), enabling composition via Task Arithmetic. The evaluation is zero-shot under a strict dual OOD protocol, and gains are concentrated on the most ambiguous samples, supporting the hypothesis that reversing information flow targets the right cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: 3D multimodal large language models (3D MLLMs) extend multimodal LLMs to understand 3D objects and scenes, often by rendering 3D data into 2D views or using point clouds. They typically recognize objects via pattern matching, which fails when objects are geometrically similar but functionally distinct (e.g., dresser vs. wardrobe). Part segmentation divides objects into meaningful parts, and functional reasoning infers an object's identity from what its parts do. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that estimates advantages by comparing rewards within a group of sampled responses, commonly used for post-training reasoning models.

## [Tokamak-SI: Unified Dataset for Interpreting Tokamak Discharges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32264) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Tokamak-SI introduces a single scalable, time-aligned, categorical dataset that consolidates labels and labeling methods from many prior studies of DIII-D tokamak discharges, covering confinement, detachment, Alfvén eigenmodes (AE), edge localized modes (ELM), sawtooth, tearing modes (TM), and resistive wall modes. Models trained on this dataset can extend labels to new shots and match or beat earlier task-specific detectors for events such as AE, ELM, and TM. This work provides a unified resource that enables task-specific benchmarking, physics-searchable archives, and direct identification of key events during experiments, which could accelerate fusion research by making it easier to compare and reuse machine learning models across different plasma phenomena. It represents a valuable step toward standardized datasets in fusion, similar to trends in other scientific domains. The dataset is built from observations on the DIII-D tokamak and includes labels for multiple event types, but the paper does not specify the size or exact composition of the dataset. The models are evaluated on their ability to extend labels to new shots and outperform earlier detectors, though the extent of improvement and generalization to other tokamaks remain to be seen.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: A tokamak is a toroidal magnetic confinement device designed to achieve controlled nuclear fusion, and DIII-D is a major tokamak operated by General Atomics in San Diego for the U.S. Department of Energy. In tokamak plasmas, various instabilities and modes occur, such as Alfvén eigenmodes (AE), edge localized modes (ELM), and tearing modes (TM), which can affect confinement and performance. Traditionally, identifying these events in discharge data has been a manual and task-specific process, with each study using its own data corpus and labeling scheme.

## [Multilingual LLMs Share Controllable Negation Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32281) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper uses causal interventions on six open multilingual LLMs to show that negation representations are largely shared across English, French, Vietnamese, and Chinese, and can be induced or removed via contrastive activation directions. The authors also find that a single sparse autoencoder (SAE) feature per model suffices to control polarity across languages. This work provides causal evidence for a language-agnostic negation mechanism in multilingual LLMs, which could inform more robust cross-lingual transfer and targeted model editing. It also demonstrates the utility of combining activation steering with sparse autoencoders for interpretability research. The study uses a synthetic contrastive dataset and a negation translation dataset derived from FLORES-200, and shows that directions extracted from one language are substantially aligned with those from others. Both dense and sparse interventions generalize to four additional languages and to FLORES-derived sentences, and data and code are released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Negation is a universal linguistic phenomenon, but its surface realization varies widely across languages. Multilingual LLMs can translate negation surprisingly well, suggesting they may learn a shared internal representation. Causal interventions, such as activation patching and steering, are techniques that modify internal model activations to test whether a representation causally affects behavior. Sparse autoencoders are tools that decompose model activations into sparse, interpretable features, and FLORES-200 is a standard multilingual translation benchmark covering 200 languages.

## [Part-DINO Combines Boundary Detection with DINO Features for Better Part Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32293) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.32293) identifies that DINO's rigid ViT patch grid causes imprecise part boundaries because patches straddling object boundaries mix pixels from multiple parts, leading to higher-entropy, lower-confidence features. The authors propose Part-DINO, a training-free framework that uses boundaries from a low-level image segmentation method to partition the image, pools DINO features within each region, and clusters them to discover parts, outperforming training-free baselines on four benchmarks. This work provides a practical, training-free solution to a known limitation of self-supervised ViTs for part discovery, which could benefit computer vision researchers working on segmentation and part-based understanding without expensive retraining. It also offers empirical evidence that DINO's invariance-based training does not fully absorb boundary pixel mixing, highlighting a fundamental issue with patch-based architectures. The analysis is based on over 26K foreground patches, showing that boundary-overlapping patches produce features with higher entropy and lower assignment confidence. Part-DINO is training-free and remains competitive with weakly-supervised and unsupervised methods across CUB, CelebA, PartImageNet-OOD, and PartImageNet-Seg, though it relies on an external boundary detector rather than improving DINO itself.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: DINO is a self-supervised Vision Transformer (ViT) that learns rich semantic features without labels, enabling tasks like object segmentation and part discovery via feature clustering. ViTs divide images into a fixed grid of patches, and each patch is treated as a token; patches that cross object boundaries contain mixed pixels, which can blur feature representations. Part discovery aims to partition a foreground object into semantic parts (e.g., a bird's head, wings, and tail) and is useful for fine-grained recognition and segmentation.

## [TNCS: Data-Efficient Backdoor Attacks on Code Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32334) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Trigger-Neutralized Counterfactual Scoring (TNCS), a method that selects which training samples to poison for data-efficient backdoor attacks on pre-trained code models. TNCS achieves higher attack success rates than baselines at very low poisoning ratios across three CodeXGLUE tasks and five trigger families, without degrading clean-task performance. This work addresses a gap in data-efficient backdoor selection methods, which have previously targeted only image classification and offer no scoring mechanism for autoregressive generation. It highlights a security risk for code models used in software development and shows that a small number of carefully chosen poisoned samples can compromise them. TNCS isolates a trigger's contribution by comparing the scoring model's output on a triggered input with its output on a trigger-neutralized counterfactual, covering both classification and generation. It is the only method effective on every trigger family tested, and its selected poison set transfers to mismatched victim models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Backdoor poisoning attacks inject crafted samples into training data so that a model misbehaves on a specific trigger while performing normally otherwise. Data-efficient attacks minimize the number of poisoned samples to reduce cost and exposure and to leave a smaller footprint for defenses. Pre-trained code models, such as those evaluated on the CodeXGLUE benchmark, are increasingly used for code generation and understanding, making their security important.

## [CONSTRAINTDIAL Builds Non-Memorizable Smart Contract Security Tasks via Protocol Mutation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32453) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose CONSTRAINTDIAL, a framework that automatically constructs diverse smart contract security tasks by mutating protocols, generating 3,045 task inputs from 203 public cases across detect, exploit, and patch categories. Compared with reconstruction alone, protocol mutation reduces success with supplied public answers by 10.9 percentage points on average, and the fraction of tasks solved by copying online answers falls by 9.3 points. This addresses a key weakness in AI agent evaluation for smart contract security: existing benchmarks can reward memorized or copied answers, including attack code recovered from public blockchain transactions. By making tasks non-memorizable, CONSTRAINTDIAL could lead to more reliable assessment of AI agents' genuine security reasoning, which matters given $38.6 billion in reported blockchain losses. The framework automates local contract reconstruction and uses attack execution to select changes to required calls or inputs, with execution checks guiding revisions that make the original attack fail while preserving exploitability, the security objective, and normal functionality. New incidents and audit findings enter the same lifelong procedure, though the work is an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Smart contracts are self-executing programs on blockchains that handle significant value, and vulnerabilities in them have led to billions in losses. AI agents are increasingly used to inspect code and run tests for security, but benchmarks that reuse public incidents can be gamed by memorization or copying published attack code. Protocol mutation here means systematically altering contract logic or interfaces to create new but still valid security challenges, similar in spirit to mutation testing in software engineering.

## [SAAF: Efficient Zero-Shot Video Anomaly Detection via Adaptive Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32486) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SAAF introduces a training-free zero-shot video anomaly detection framework that combines a dual-signal adaptive sampler, a threshold-gated cascaded architecture, and signal-aware weighted fusion. It sets new state-of-the-art results on XD-Violence (92.07%), MSAD (86.70%), and ShanghaiTech (80.54%) AUC-ROC among training-free zero-shot methods, while achieving 2.2–2.6× higher throughput than the prior best zero-shot approach. Deploying Vision-Language Models for video anomaly detection is computationally expensive, and SAAF's efficiency gains make real-time, large-scale deployment more feasible without requiring labeled training data. Its ability to surpass supervised baselines on two benchmarks suggests zero-shot methods can be competitive in practical surveillance and monitoring applications. The dual-signal sampler uses a delta signal (frame-to-frame cosine distance) for sudden events and a drift signal (deviation from rolling temporal context) for gradual anomalies, combined with OR-gating and adaptive EMA-based thresholding. The cascaded architecture invokes expensive video-level temporal analysis only when lightweight frame-level scoring yields sufficient confidence, and the fusion mechanism adapts score combination based on which signal triggered selection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Video anomaly detection aims to identify unusual events in video streams, a key task for surveillance and safety monitoring. Zero-shot methods use pre-trained models like Vision-Language Models to detect anomalies without task-specific training data, but their semantic reasoning is computationally heavy. SAAF addresses this by selectively applying expensive analysis only when needed, using adaptive sampling and cascaded processing to reduce cost.

## [InferSlice: SLO-driven LLM inference over telco WAN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32638) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InferSlice, a reference architecture led by BT with the University of Bristol and NVIDIA, integrates network slicing, GPU partitioning, elastic serving, and observability to enable SLO-driven LLM inference over a telco wide-area network. It was evaluated on the UK JOINER national-scale WAN, examining service differentiation, elastic scale-out, bandwidth bottlenecks, and the reach of tensor, pipeline, and data parallelism plus prefill/decode disaggregation. This work addresses the gap between centralized cloud LLM serving and geographically distributed telco infrastructure, showing how operators can offer differentiated, SLO-compliant inference services. It provides practical guidance for deploying LLMs across wide-area networks, which could impact enterprises with sovereignty, compliance, or latency constraints. Results show that tiers yield distinct latency-throughput regimes, weight streaming shortens scale-out time, replication is placement-insensitive but memory-bound, pipeline parallelism tolerates near- and mid-cross-site hops, while tensor parallelism and disaggregation fail beyond a single subnet hop. These findings highlight the sensitivity of different parallelism strategies to WAN latency and the importance of network-aware placement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM inference is typically served from centralized data centers, but sovereignty, compliance, latency, and resource-locality requirements can make centralized deployments unsuitable. Telco operators own geographically distributed compute and network infrastructure, yet existing LLM serving frameworks are designed for tightly coupled data centers and lack support for coordinating heterogeneous, distributed resources. Network slicing allows operators to create multiple virtual networks with distinct QoS on a shared 5G infrastructure, while GPU partitioning and elastic serving help allocate resources efficiently. InferSlice explores how these techniques can be combined to deliver SLO-driven LLM inference over a wide-area network.

## [SemGeo-Gen: Unsupervised Generation of Cross-Instance 3D Semantic-Geometric Correspondences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32663) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SemGeo-Gen introduces an unsupervised method that generates approximate cross-instance semantic-geometric correspondences from weakly aligned 3D object collections, using multi-view DINOv2 features lifted to 3D, continuous piecewise-affine registration, and semantic consistency pruning. The generated correspondences achieve 93% PCK@0.10 and 97% PCK@0.15 against KeypointNet annotations, and pretraining with them improves state-of-the-art semantic correspondence models on SPair-71k by up to 5 PCK@0.10 points after fine-tuning. This work offers a scalable alternative to costly manual annotation for training 3D-3D, 3D-2D, and 2D-2D correspondence models, potentially lowering the barrier for building large-scale supervision in 3D vision. It demonstrates that approximate supervision from synthetic 3D assets can transfer to real-image correspondence learning, which could benefit applications in robotics, AR/VR, and 3D content creation. The method only requires coarse category-level rotation alignment and does not aim for exact point-level correspondences, which are often ambiguous across instances. The generated 3D correspondences can be projected into rendered views to produce 3D-3D, 3D-2D, and 2D-2D supervision without manual annotation, and the approach is validated against sparse human annotations from KeypointNet.

## [Internal Discrepancy Measure Unifies Contrastive Learning Generalization Bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32729) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a new measure called internal discrepancy, defined as the difference between the mean representation of positive samples and latent cluster centers, and proves a generalization bound for downstream supervised tasks after contrastive learning without additional assumptions. This bound unifies existing assumptions and motivates a relaxed k-means loss to reduce the discrepancy. This work provides a unifying theoretical framework for contrastive learning, connecting various prior assumptions and offering a principled motivation for a new loss function. It could impact the design of contrastive learning methods and our understanding of why they work, especially in imbalanced settings. The internal discrepancy-aware bound leads to a relaxed k-means loss as an approximation, and the paper also analyzes downstream behaviors via contrastive learning-incurred supervised losses, explaining why contrastive learning helps in class imbalance and proving properties about the optimality of these losses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Contrastive learning is a popular self-supervised approach that trains representations by pulling positive pairs together and pushing negative pairs apart. This paper proposes a measure to encapsulate those assumptions and derive a bound without them.

## [AWARE: Camera-Only World Model Detects and Localizes Robot Anomalies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32742) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present AWARE, a world-model framework that detects and localizes anomalies in robotic systems using only external camera monitoring, without onboard sensors. Evaluated on 240 real-world robotic crane trajectories including payload collisions and motor degradations, it achieves a 26% improvement in anomaly classification over the strongest baseline and localizes the degrading motor with 88% accuracy. This approach enables anomaly detection on legacy or inaccessible hardware where onboard sensors are missing or security protocols prohibit direct access, broadening predictive maintenance to critical infrastructure such as cranes. It also shows that world models can support introspection by decoding inferred dynamics parameters to identify fault sources, not just flag anomalies. AWARE estimates system dynamics and predicts future states from noisy camera-only observations, then decodes the inferred dynamics parameter to localize faults. The evaluation covers 240 real-world crane trajectories with payload collisions and motor degradations, though results are limited to this single robotic platform and camera-based state estimation remains noisy.

**Background**: World models are learned internal representations that let a system predict how its environment will evolve given current observations and actions, and they are increasingly used in robot learning for dynamics prediction and control. Traditional anomaly detection in robotics often relies on onboard sensors such as motor encoders, which legacy equipment may lack or security policies may block. AWARE instead uses external cameras to perform state estimation and dynamics prediction, enabling anomaly detection and fault localization without direct system access.

Researchers introduced VERID (VERified Infrastructure for Discovery Laboratories), a persistent, high-fidelity semantic digital twin of a laboratory workcell that provides queryable introspection, time-compressed simulation, trace persistence, and safe execution for AI agents. By enabling safe and inexpensive in silico exploration of experimental designs, it addresses a key bottleneck in translating AI-generated hypotheses into reliable physical experiments. VERID functions as a discrete accounting engine for simulation, allowing agents to explore experimental designs in silico and refine strategies through iterative self-play. It also captures structured multimodal experimental data for downstream analysis, knowledge mining, and agent training, though validation so far is limited to simple aqueous dye and water mixtures.

A new arXiv paper presents a pipeline that builds a knowledge graph of customer and integration relations for healthcare software from the open web, enforcing that every edge must be supported by a verbatim quote from its source page. The pipeline combines a deterministic crawl of vendor sites with a budgeted ReAct agent for broader web search, and uses a permissive extractor plus a layered grounding stage to achieve 0.94 precision and 0.71 recall on 61 vendors. This work addresses the critical issue of correctness and verifiability in automatically extracted claims, which is especially important in healthcare where a wrongly listed customer can mislead buyers and misrepresent an organization. The quote-grounding approach and the use of bounded model calls with voting could be adapted to other domains requiring trustworthy information extraction from the web. The grounding stage includes a referent step that reads the whole page when a quote names only one side, deterministic checks, and a panel of five model calls voting on nine criteria. A confidence score built from the panel's votes outperforms a fixed rule, reaching 0.90 precision at 0.90 recall, and the authors discuss how narrow, bounded model calls and string-matched evidence mitigate long-context degradation and run-to-run variance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs represent entities and their relationships as nodes and edges, and are used to organize complex information. Language-model agents, such as those using the ReAct pattern, combine reasoning and acting to perform tasks like web search and information extraction. In healthcare software procurement, buyers need evidence of which peer institutions use a product and what systems it integrates with, but this evidence is scattered across vendor websites, press releases, and third-party pages.

## [Multiplicative State Coupling Enables Out-of-Distribution Generalization in Learned MAX-CUT Schedules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32801) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.32801) shows that a bilinear (multiplicative) state-adaptive schedule for a learned MAX-CUT solver generalizes out of distribution, outperforming its untrained baseline across 15 seeds (t=4.1), while an additive ablation with identical inputs and output heads fails out of distribution (t=-11.7) despite being statistically indistinguishable in distribution. This finding isolates how solver state enters the schedule—multiplicative versus additive coupling—as the key architectural distinction for out-of-distribution transfer, which could inform the design of more robust learned optimizers and schedules for combinatorial optimization and beyond. The separation appears without regularization and persists across a 30-round weight-decay sweep, with the bilinear advantage remaining significant at every tested value while the additive model never recovers; the study focuses on a continuous penalty relaxation of MAX-CUT and backpropagates through an unrolled classical solver.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: MAX-CUT is a classic NP-hard combinatorial optimization problem, often tackled via continuous relaxations that allow gradient-based methods. Learned optimizers use neural networks to generate update rules or schedules, and backpropagating through an unrolled solver (similar to backpropagation through time) enables training such schedules end-to-end. Out-of-distribution generalization refers to performance on instances larger or different from those seen during training, a key challenge for learned optimization methods.

## [TopKLoRA: Sparse fine-tuning enables faithful circuit discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32866) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TopKLoRA is a new interpretable-by-design LoRA variant that activates only the k largest adapter latents per token, trained purely on downstream loss. In a sleeper-agent backdoor case study on gemma-2-2b, Qwen2.5-1.5B, and Qwen2.5-7B, it identified and ablated circuits necessary and sufficient for the backdoor, producing sparse circuits up to an order of magnitude smaller than dense LoRA baselines. This work bridges parameter-efficient fine-tuning and mechanistic interpretability, offering a practical way to localize and remove malicious capabilities like backdoors without sacrificing instruction-following performance. It could impact AI safety research and the deployment of fine-tuned models by enabling surgical ablation of harmful behaviors. Unlike sparse autoencoders (SAEs), TopKLoRA is trained only on the downstream loss, which incentivizes learning functionally causal latents. The authors also propose a technique to construct model organisms with localized circuits using TopKLoRA latents and gradient-routing to validate their circuit search algorithm, and they release code on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that adds small low-rank matrices to a model, but the resulting changes are hard to interpret. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a common tool for interpretability, but they are trained separately from the downstream task. A backdoor is a hidden behavior implanted during fine-tuning on poisoned data, and a sleeper agent is a model with such a backdoor that activates only under specific triggers. Circuit discovery is a mechanistic interpretability approach that identifies subnetworks responsible for specific behaviors.

## [LLM Code Optimization May Incur More Energy Than It Saves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32891) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper empirically evaluates whether LLM self-refinement pipelines for code optimization yield net energy savings, measuring hardware-level energy across 11 open-source models and 1,000 EffiBench problems. It finds inference energy of 0.79–8.68 MJ and that 42 of 44 configurations produce code that consumes more energy per problem than canonical human reference solutions, with overheads from +4% to +886%. This challenges the common assumption that faster code from LLM optimization automatically reduces energy use, showing that inference costs can dominate and that performance and energy efficiency are not always aligned. It has implications for sustainable AI practices and for developers relying on LLM self-refinement for green software engineering. Limiting maximum output tokens reduces inference energy by up to 69% compared to default settings, and Mixture-of-Experts architectures consume substantially less inference energy than equivalently sized dense models without sacrificing correctness. The two configurations that did save energy only achieved marginal gains, with break-even points on the order of 10^6 to 10^7 executions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: EffiBench is a benchmark of 1,000 efficiency-critical coding problems designed to evaluate the runtime efficiency of code generated by LLMs. LLM self-refinement pipelines iteratively prompt a model to improve its own code, and it is often assumed that faster execution translates into lower energy consumption. This paper measures both inference energy and execution energy to test that assumption end-to-end.

## [HAD-VLM blends autoregressive text and diffusion for continuous medical measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32948) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced HAD-VLM, a hybrid autoregressive-diffusion vision-language model that predicts continuous ultrasound measurements (caliper endpoints and length) via a diffusion process while generating text autoregressively in a shared backbone. On EchoNet-LVH echocardiography tasks, it achieved mean absolute errors of 1.36, 2.77, 3.05, and 1.16 mm for IVSd, LVIDd, LVIDs, and LVPWd, reducing errors by 21.4–37.6% versus a Vicuna-based autoregressive baseline and outperforming fine-tuned Qwen3-VL and direct MLP regression. Discrete tokenization is a fundamental bottleneck for numerical prediction in vision-language models, and this hybrid approach shows that continuous diffusion heads can be added without sacrificing prompt-conditioned language interaction. If validated more broadly, it could improve quantitative measurement workflows in ultrasound, X-ray, and other medical imaging where precise numbers matter clinically. The diffusion head predicts a five-component representation (caliper endpoint coordinates plus length), refined by a coordinate-length consistency mechanism and ensemble prediction; visual prompt tuning adapts the vision encoder to ultrasound. Medical question answering performance is preserved on the evaluated dataset, and on X-ray vertebral measurement the mean absolute error drops from 11.4 to 6.7 pixels, though this is a specialized arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models typically generate text token by token, and numbers are represented as discrete tokens, which poorly captures continuous quantities like a caliper measurement in millimeters. Diffusion models, by contrast, learn to generate continuous values by iteratively denoising, and hybrid autoregressive-diffusion designs have recently been explored in robotics and multimodal generation. HAD-VLM applies this idea to medical imaging, where tasks such as echocardiographic chamber measurement require both language interaction and anatomically precise numerical output.

## [DexPort: Portable Hand-Held Interface for Dexterous Robot Hand Data Collection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32969) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DexPort introduces a portable hand-held data-collection interface for high-DoF robot hands that uses the same hand hardware for both collection and deployment, recording commanded joint targets and measured joint states. Operators move the interface to specify wrist motion and select skills to coordinate finger motion, enabling collection of over 2,500 demonstrations across four real-world tasks without a robot arm. This addresses a central bottleneck in dexterous manipulation learning by eliminating the embodiment gap between demonstration collection and policy deployment, while also removing the need for a full robot setup during data collection. The reported gains—success rates improving from 11% to 64% with recorded commands and from 36% to 78% with in-the-wild data—suggest the approach could make high-quality demonstration collection more scalable and accessible. The system records both commanded joint targets and measured joint states, and behavior cloning policies are trained directly on wrist motions and hand commands without skill labels. The evaluation covers pick-and-place with diverse objects, screwing with in-hand tool use, drawer stowage requiring simultaneous grasp and pulling, and a bimanual scanning task, with data collected in both laboratory and in-the-wild settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Collecting high-quality demonstrations is a major bottleneck for learning dexterous manipulation. Teleoperation records executable robot actions but requires a complete robot setup and burdens the operator with coordinating many finger joints, while human demonstrations are easier to collect but require motion estimation and retargeting to the robot and do not directly provide robot actions. DexPort aims to bridge these approaches by using the same hand hardware for collection and deployment, with skill-based finger coordination to simplify demonstration collection.

RobustRLlib is a new open-source library and benchmark that integrates 17 representative robust online, offline, and safe reinforcement learning algorithms under a unified interface, and evaluates them across 273 perturbation configurations on diverse real-world shifts and advanced humanoid and vision-language-action tasks.
