## [HarnessMM Cuts Agent Token Costs via Guarded Decision Reuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32813) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.32813) introduces HarnessMM, a method that combines guarded decision reuse with recursive self-improvement (RSI) to reduce redundant reasoning callbacks in agent harnesses. On HumanEval, it preserves Codex success at 90.82% while cutting tokens per task from 66.72k to 47.02k (29.54% saving), and on DeepSeek Harness it raises success from 71.43% to 85.71% while reducing tokens per task from 96.28k to 11.62k (87.93% saving). Token efficiency is a major cost driver for LLM agents that repeatedly call reasoning models after tool feedback, so a mechanism that reuses already-resolved decisions without retraining the actor could substantially lower inference costs for existing harnesses. The approach also suggests a practical, bounded form of self-improvement that refines harness configuration rather than model weights. The system stores exact actions, literal arguments, causal dependencies, and public guards in Executable Decision Graphs; Guarded Decision Reuse activates them from observed receipts, and Certified Callback Substitution executes them through the native interface while recording removal of the registered slow callback. New semantic decisions, unsupported evidence, and selector abstention fall back to the original actor, and an MBPP ablation shows 26.91% lower cost than single-use graphs and 19.52% lower cost than retained callbacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness (or scaffold) is the runtime software around an LLM that manages tool use, memory, state, and feedback loops, turning model outputs into actions. In such harnesses, the model is often invoked again after each tool result even when the next action is already determined, which wastes tokens. Recursive self-improvement generally refers to systems that iteratively improve their own components; here it is applied narrowly to refining harness configurations from training feedback, with validation selecting the deployed configuration.

## [LLM agents violate complement consistency in opinion simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32874) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper tests three small open models (Qwen3-4B, Llama-3.2-3B, Gemma-3-4B) and finds that when a claim is rewritten as its complement with probabilities p replaced by 1-p, the paired probability reports often sum to more than one, averaging 1.18 for Qwen on 2,304 agent states from a running network simulation. The failure also appears on 48 real fact-checking claims from AVeriTeC and in a controlled benchmark of 24 two-outcome worlds. If simulated agents' probability reports do not respect basic complement consistency, then treating those numbers as beliefs in opinion-dynamics studies is questionable, which undermines a growing body of LLM-agent social simulation research. The finding suggests that consistency under rewording needs its own test before simulated probabilities are interpreted as opinions. The discrepancy is two to ten times larger than the change produced by a same-meaning paraphrase and persists under repeated sampling and across peer group sizes and report strengths; when fed back into two populations starting from complementary states, the populations diverge after five rounds for every model. Averaging each answer with the complement of its paired answer restores consistency by construction and lowers Brier loss on average, but at the final round it beats the better of the two original wordings for only one of the three models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-agent opinion simulations model each agent as reporting a probability that a claim is true, then updating based on neighbors' reports. Complement consistency is the logical requirement that a claim and its negation receive probabilities summing to one; if an agent's number represents its position rather than the specific sentence shown, rewriting the claim as its complement and replacing p with 1-p should yield the complementary answer. AVeriTeC is a dataset of 4,568 real-world claims from 50 fact-checking organizations used to evaluate automated claim verification.

## [Astro2Method benchmark tests LLMs on ML method selection in astronomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30282) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced Astro2Method, a benchmark of 500 expert-reviewed binary questions drawn from 346 astronomy papers published between January 2024 and June 2026, to test whether LLMs can choose suitable machine learning methods for scientific problems. Across five LLMs, direct prompting achieved 87.50–92.55% accuracy on Easy questions but only 58.87–64.52% on Hard questions, and retrieval-augmented generation and agent methods such as ReAct, Self-Ask, and CRITIC did not bring consistent improvement. As machine learning becomes more common in astronomy, method selection often requires combined expertise in both fields, and this benchmark shows that current LLMs are reliable only on straightforward cases. The findings caution against assuming that retrieval or agentic reasoning automatically improves LLM advice on complex scientific method choices. The benchmark uses binary questions based on clear performance differences, with Easy questions having complete author explanations and Hard questions only partial explanations; providing the source paper's pipeline description with method names masked improved Hard accuracy to match or exceed web search. Agents required to search the web improved mainly when they found the exact source papers, with no consistent gain otherwise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used as assistants in scientific workflows, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) lets them pull in external documents to ground their answers. Agent frameworks such as ReAct combine chain-of-thought reasoning with tool use, while Self-Ask and CRITIC add sub-question decomposition and self-correction. This paper tests whether such techniques help LLMs advise astronomers on which ML method fits a given research problem.

## [Graph-Based Reasoning Preserves Ambiguity in Structured Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31681) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.31681) reformulates structured generation as an ambiguity-aware inference problem, where the goal is to reason over a set of plausible structured outputs instead of predicting a single solution. The authors propose a multi-valid learning framework that constructs a candidate space of structured outputs, represents each candidate as a graph of entities and relationships, and selects via graph-based reasoning over semantic alignment and relational consistency. This work challenges the deterministic single-output paradigm in structured generation, which often collapses ambiguity and leads to unstable outputs under underspecified inputs. If validated, the approach could improve robustness and consistency for NLP applications like information extraction and semantic parsing, where multiple valid interpretations exist. The paper shows that standard training inherently suppresses valid alternatives, and its graph-based selection enables principled comparison beyond token-level likelihoods. Experiments on structured generation benchmarks report consistent gains in accuracy, stability, and robustness under varying ambiguity levels, though the abstract is truncated and no code or community discussion is provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Structured generation refers to tasks where a model must produce structured outputs such as graphs, tables, or parse trees from natural language input. Traditional methods use single-reference supervision, training models to map each input to one correct output, but natural language often admits multiple structurally distinct yet semantically valid interpretations. Ambiguity-aware inference is an emerging direction that aims to handle such ambiguity explicitly rather than forcing a single prediction.

## [Agentic Framework Enables Traceable Multimodal Emotion Reasoning Without Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31685) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.31685) proposes an agentic framework for multimodal emotion reasoning that uses modality-specific perception agents, a shared evidence board, and an evidence-grounded verification stage to trace errors and adapt prompts without updating model weights. On the MER2025 dataset, targeted prompt adaptation improved F-score by 0.6–2.2 points across four frozen MLLMs (VideoLLaMA3-2B, Phi-3.5-vision-instruct, Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct, and InternVL2.5-38B), including a rise from 52.3 to 54.5 on Qwen2.5-VL-7B. This work addresses a key challenge in agentic pipelines built from frozen models: identifying which component caused an error. By making failures traceable and adapting prompts in a targeted, validated way, it offers a cost-effective alternative to instruction tuning for affective computing and multimodal AI systems. The framework uses deterministic checks to verify that cited evidence and references are valid, plus an entailment check to confirm final claims are supported by evidence; detected failures are grouped by responsible component and failure type, converted into prompt patches, and accepted only after validation on a held-out set. The approach matches or outperforms undifferentiated reflection baselines on all four backbones.

Agentic pipelines composed of frozen models avoid tuning costs but make it hard to trace errors to specific stages. This paper introduces evidence-grounded verification and prompt-space adaptation to address that traceability problem.

## [Poly-Ripple: Collaborative In-Context Learning for Indoor Radio Map Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31702) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes Poly-Ripple, a framework that formulates indoor radio map estimation (RME) as a collaborative in-context learning (ICL) problem, where multiple clients use large autoregressive vision models (LAVMs) without any model updates. It introduces ripple-ordered autoregressive modeling to capture the causal structure of wireless propagation and importance-aware aggregation to combine heterogeneous contextual knowledge across clients under context length constraints. This work addresses two key challenges in indoor RME: the need for costly labeled data and task-specific training, and privacy concerns around sensitive building layouts that cannot be centralized. By enabling privacy-preserving, training-free estimation, it could make radio map deployment more practical for coverage planning, localization, and network deployment in indoor environments. The method relies on prompting each client with its private demonstrations to predict the radio map for the same scene query, and uses importance-aware aggregation to handle heterogeneous contextual knowledge under context length limits. The paper provides theoretical analysis and experiments on a public indoor RME benchmark, showing consistent outperformance over representative ICL baselines, though it remains an early-stage arXiv preprint without community validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Radio map estimation (RME) is the process of reconstructing spatial radio metrics (such as signal strength) from sparse measurements, and is important for wireless coverage planning and localization. In-context learning (ICL) allows models to adapt to new tasks at inference time by conditioning on a few demonstration examples in the prompt, without any parameter updates. Large autoregressive vision models generate images by predicting tokens sequentially, and here they are repurposed to predict radio maps in a ripple order outward from the transmitter.

## [On-premises agentic LLM assistant combines RAG and fine-tuned Llama-3-8B for HPC help desk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31703) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents an on-premises agentic LLM assistant that answers HPC user questions by orchestrating two tools over the Model Context Protocol: RAG over live documentation and a QLoRA-fine-tuned Llama-3-8B model trained on 11,244 historical ticket resolutions. Evaluated on 400 held-out tickets from Purdue's Rosen Center for Advanced Computing, the fine-tuned tool improved BERTScore F1 from 0.06 to 0.27 over the base model, but the end-to-end merged system did not outperform its best individual tool. This work shows a practical, privacy-conscious path for deploying LLM assistants in sensitive institutional settings where ticket data cannot leave the premises, and it offers a candid negative result: merging complementary tools is harder than it looks. HPC centers and IT help desks considering similar assistants can learn that fine-tuning on rewritten resolutions is the highest-value component, while naive answer merging may dilute quality. The system runs entirely on-premises or on the university's hosted LLM service due to data restrictions, and evaluation used two judges from model families that generated none of the answers. The oracle bound (choosing the better answer per question) reached 3.41 judged correctness versus 3.20 for the best tool, but the merge followed the documentation tool in 78% of answers, and neither a learned selector nor an LLM verifier beat the fine-tuned tool alone (3.19 versus 2.66).

**Background**: HPC help desks receive many questions already answered in user guides or past tickets, but users struggle to navigate documentation and cannot search others' tickets. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) lets an LLM ground answers in retrieved documents, while QLoRA is an efficient fine-tuning method that trains low-rank adapters on a 4-bit quantized model, making it feasible to adapt an 8B-parameter Llama-3 model on modest hardware. The Model Context Protocol (MCP), introduced by Anthropic in late 2024, is an open standard for connecting LLM applications to external tools and data sources.

## [RubricCraft: Generating Evaluation Rubrics from Divergent Solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31707) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RubricCraft introduces a method that constructs evaluation rubrics for open-ended language agent tasks by comparing multiple independently sampled solver trajectories, surfacing requirements and failure modes that task descriptions alone miss. On SpreadsheetBench v2, its rubrics passed only 29.5% of wrong outputs versus 42.6% for a prompt-only baseline, while failing none of the outputs accepted by the benchmark's own checker. This addresses a real pain point in agent benchmarking: model-based graders are inconsistent and exhaustive programmatic checks are impractical, so better rubrics could make evaluation of open-ended, artifact-producing agent tasks more reliable. It may influence how benchmarks like SpreadsheetBench are designed and how agent outputs are graded in practice. The method iteratively revises criteria by applying them to collected solutions and fixing checks that overlook errors or penalize valid alternatives; repair driven by attempts from three model families acted on different tasks and an evidence audit judged most repairs as improvements, many on tasks whose reference solution is itself wrong. A caveat is that repair on a single attempt left grading unchanged.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Language agents are LLM-powered systems that perform multi-step tasks and produce artifacts such as spreadsheets or code. Evaluating them on open-ended tasks is hard: model-based graders (another LLM scoring outputs) can be inconsistent, while programmatic checks are often impractical. Textual rubrics make criteria explicit but traditionally require domain expertise to write, and rubrics generated from task descriptions alone can miss failure modes that only appear in attempted solutions.

## [Graph-Text Templates Enable Selective Reuse of Mobile GUI Agent Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31712) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a graph-text template framework that selectively reuses execution traces in mobile GUI agents by validating individual UI transitions against the current instruction and live interface state. On AndroidWorld, it reduces LLM inference token consumption by about 80% on average across five base agents, with most agents achieving at least 1.5x execution speedup. Mobile GUI agents often repeat similar interaction procedures, so cutting redundant LLM inference could make them faster and cheaper to deploy at scale. The approach matters for developers building on-device or cloud-based automation agents where latency and token cost are key constraints. The framework characterizes variations into six representative categories and uses a record-template-replay pipeline to reuse, adapt, or discard individual transitions rather than whole procedures. Real-device evaluations on Android and HarmonyOS report action reuse ratios of 70.7–79.8% while maintaining over 2x speedup in most configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Mobile GUI agents are LLM-driven systems that operate smartphone apps by perceiving screen states and issuing actions such as taps and swipes. Because they typically call a large language model at every step, repeated tasks incur high inference latency and token costs. Trace reuse aims to replay previously successful action sequences, but user instructions and interface states can change, invalidating naive replay. Graph-text templates combine a graph of UI states and transitions with text encoding task intent and variable slots to make reuse more robust.

## [LLMMAD: LLM-based autoregressive multimodal anomaly detection for microservices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31722) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces LLMMAD, an autoregressive multimodal anomaly detection framework for microservice systems that uses Large Language Models to model inter-modality relationships while preserving modality-specific features through modality-wise reconstruction heads. It also addresses the identity-shortcut problem by employing a prediction-driven autoregressive objective that predicts future states instead of copying current inputs, achieving F1-score improvements of 3.1%, 1.4%, and 0.9% over state-of-the-art methods on three real-world datasets. Microservice architectures are dominant in large-scale cloud applications, and failures can propagate quickly across services, so more reliable anomaly detection directly improves system availability. LLMMAD's approach of combining cross-modal fusion with modality-specific preservation and avoiding trivial reconstruction shortcuts could influence how multimodal monitoring systems are designed for production environments. LLMMAD embeds and recombines multimodal data into a unified sequence for LLM processing to capture inter-modality relationships, while modality-wise reconstruction heads retain modality-specific information. The prediction-driven autoregressive objective eliminates the identity shortcut by forcing the model to predict future states rather than copy inputs, though the evaluation is limited to three real-world datasets and the gains are incremental.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Microservice architecture organizes an application into loosely coupled, independently deployable services that communicate via lightweight protocols, which enables rapid deployment and elastic scaling but also allows failures to propagate across services. Anomaly detection in such systems often uses multimodal data such as logs, metrics, and traces, and reconstruction-based methods can suffer from the identity-shortcut problem where the model trivially copies inputs to outputs instead of learning meaningful representations. Large Language Models have recently been applied to sequence modeling tasks beyond text, including system telemetry analysis.

## [Generalized green and red AI frameworks extend sustainability taxonomy to big data and ICT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31739) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes generalized green AI (GGAI) and generalized red AI (GRAI) frameworks, extending the green/red taxonomy to red big data (RBD), red ICT (RICT), and red electronic and electrical engineering (REEE) under a unified Environmental-Economic-Social-Technical/Operational (E/Ec/S/T) framework. It analyzes footprint indicators like electricity, carbon, water, and e-waste across AI-BD-ICT stacks and organizes countermeasures into a four-tier taxonomy. This work broadens the sustainability discussion beyond energy efficiency and carbon emissions, addressing lifecycle trade-offs and socio-economic impacts that are often overlooked. It could influence how researchers, policymakers, and industry assess and mitigate the environmental footprint of AI and digital technologies. The paper cites projections that data centers could consume 6.7–12% of U.S. electricity by 2028 (up from 4.4% in 2023) and AI-related water demand could reach 4–6 billion m³ annually by 2027. It proposes metric-level reform, design principle revisions, efficiency-oriented LLM practices, and energy infrastructure transition as actionable countermeasures, but provides no empirical validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Green AI typically refers to AI research and deployment that prioritizes energy efficiency and low carbon emissions, while red AI emphasizes maximizing performance regardless of computational cost. As AI models grow larger, concerns have risen about their environmental footprint, including electricity use, water consumption for cooling, and electronic waste. This paper argues that existing green/red AI discussions are too narrow and proposes a more comprehensive framework.

## [Query Abstraction Governs BM25 vs Dense Retrieval in Classical Arabic Poetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31821) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper benchmarks nine dense encoders and BM25 on Classical Arabic poetry retrieval across two corpora, three indexing modes, and two evaluation protocols. It finds that BM25 leads on tip-of-the-tongue queries (0.746 vs. 0.717 Recall@5 for an 8B dense model) but drops to eighth place on abstract queries (0.392 vs. 0.748). The results show that retrieval performance depends heavily on how much of the original wording a query retains, not just on model quality, which challenges the assumption that larger dense models always win. This has implications for search systems over literary and cultural archives, where users often remember fragments rather than full text. Editing query wording shows the ranking is largely driven by query composition: adding one remembered half-verse raises BM25 by 38 points, and rewriting quoted words by hand leaves BM25 level with the 8B encoder. The two standard evaluation protocols, known-item retrieval and thematic relevance, dissociate on the same systems, meaning a benchmark reporting only one protocol can give a materially wrong picture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: BM25 is a classic lexical ranking function that scores documents by term overlap, while dense retrieval encodes queries and documents into vectors and ranks by embedding similarity. Recall@5 measures whether the correct document appears among the top five results. Classical Arabic poetry presents a challenging retrieval domain because users often remember only fragments of verses, leading to queries with varying levels of abstraction.

## [Offline Multilingual LLM Assistance via ISO 11783 Manual Sync](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31863) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper analyzes the semantic synchronization of machine-readable operator manuals over ISO 11783 CAN buses to enable offline, multilingual LLM assistance for agricultural machinery. It benchmarks data transfer performance using Markdown, JSON, and XML formats and evaluates open-weight LLMs on cross-lingual question answering, validating results against four human raters. This work demonstrates a concrete integration of LLMs into embedded agricultural systems, potentially enabling intelligent offline assistance without cloud connectivity. It proposes a new parameter group that leverages existing ISO 11783 mechanisms, making it a candidate for standardization and inspiring similar applications in other domains. A complete manual was transferred over a physical ISO 11783 network in 31 to 89 seconds, comparable to the object pool upload operators already wait for at first coupling. Markdown produced the smallest files, and with retrieval-augmented generation, locally run models answered most English manual questions correctly, with no advantage of the largest model over 7B and 8B models, but performed worse on five other language editions; keyword retrieval failed across languages, whereas semantic retrieval provided cross-lingual capability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: ISO 11783, also known as ISOBUS, is an international standard for communication between agricultural tractors and implements over a CAN bus. It defines protocols for control and data exchange, including object pools that contain implement descriptions and are uploaded to the tractor terminal at first connection. Open-weight LLMs are models whose parameters are publicly available, allowing local deployment and fine-tuning; retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines a retriever to fetch relevant documents with an LLM to generate answers, enabling offline operation on local knowledge bases.

## [RobustNCL: Lightweight Detect-and-Cure for Fault-Tolerant Neuromorphic Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31954) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes RobustNCL, a framework that monitors weight data accessed from off-chip DRAM for anomalous signatures caused by transient faults and cures corrupted weights using one of three variants: zero replacement, min/max value, or most-probable value from the clean weight distribution. Experimental results show that under a high fault rate of 5×10⁻⁴ for memory replay, the best RobustNCL variant stays within 0.5% accuracy of fault-free models across Split MNIST, Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100, with negligible latency and energy overhead. This work addresses a previously unexplored reliability gap in neuromorphic continual learning, showing that transient hardware faults from particle strikes can be mitigated cheaply without sacrificing the energy efficiency that makes NCL attractive. It could enable more dependable deployment of always-learning neuromorphic systems in edge and embedded environments where radiation-induced errors are a concern. The curing values are stored in radiation-hardened memory to support the detect-and-cure strategy, and the approach is evaluated across multiple NCL methods and datasets. The paper reports that the best variant maintains accuracy within -0.5% of fault-free models, but the evaluation focuses on transient faults in off-chip DRAM rather than other hardware components.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Neuromorphic continual learning (NCL) combines continual learning with spiking neural networks to let deployed systems learn new data at runtime with sparse, event-based operations that are highly energy-efficient. However, hardware platforms are susceptible to transient faults—temporary bit flips caused by high-energy particle strikes—which can corrupt weights stored in off-chip DRAM and degrade NCL performance. Prior to this work, the reliability of NCL under such faults had not been studied, and no fault-tolerance techniques existed for this setting.

## [ACVA-Bench: New Benchmark Tests T2I Models on Abstract Concept Visualization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32058) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce ACVA-Bench, a category-balanced benchmark grounded in psycholinguistic norms that evaluates text-to-image models' ability to visualize abstract concepts like "loyalty" along three dimensions: semantic sufficiency, representational diversity, and conceptual distinctiveness. The benchmark uses a low-intervention setting that withholds concrete objects, scenes, or visual metaphors, and employs a fine-grained visual-semantic evaluation method that decomposes concept semantics into independently verifiable questions, validated by human evaluation. This benchmark addresses a gap beyond literal prompt following, providing an interpretable and reproducible evaluation foundation for assessing whether T2I models can autonomously translate abstract concepts into accurate, diverse, and distinguishable visual representations. It could drive improvements in model creativity and deep understanding, affecting researchers and developers working on generative AI evaluation. Since abstract concepts lack a unique reference image, conventional reference-matching metrics are difficult to apply; the proposed method instead uses concept-semantics-constrained visual-semantic questions. Benchmarking mainstream T2I models reveals they rely on a small set of prototypical visual patterns, leaving room for improvement in representational diversity and conceptual distinctiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image models have made great progress in image quality and instruction following, but it remains unclear whether they can deeply understand abstract concepts that have no fixed visual referent. Psycholinguistic norms study how humans represent abstract concepts across dimensions like concreteness and imageability, and ACVA-Bench draws on these norms to design its evaluation categories. The benchmark defines Abstract Concept Visualization Ability (ACVA) as the capacity to generate visual representations that are semantically sufficient, diverse, and distinct from related concepts.

## [XAVAD: Explainable, Privacy-Preserving Zero-Shot Video Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32073) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces XAVAD, a unified descriptive explainability framework for anonymized video anomaly detection that combines zero-shot anonymization, anomaly type categorization, visual localization, and scene context description. It uses multimodal CLIP embeddings with improved LLM-assisted prompting and pool-scoring for anomaly-type identification, enhances Grad-ECLIP for spatial heatmap localization via a prompt-guided patching autofocus mechanism, and employs VLMs for context and reasoning descriptions. This work addresses the underexplored intersection of explainable AI, video anomaly detection, and privacy-preserving anonymization, which is important for building trust and transparency in safety and security monitoring systems. It could facilitate adoption of computer vision systems in sensitive surveillance contexts by providing multifaceted explanations while protecting privacy. The approach is evaluated on several publicly available CCTV and video datasets, showing substantial gains over state-of-the-art benchmarks, and the authors present ablation studies and privacy protection trade-off insights. However, as an arXiv preprint with a truncated abstract, the full empirical impact and limitations are not fully assessable from the summary alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Video anomaly detection (VAD) aims to automatically identify unusual events in video streams, which is critical for surveillance but raises privacy concerns. Zero-shot learning allows models to recognize classes not seen during training by leveraging auxiliary information, while CLIP is a multimodal model that learns joint text-image embeddings and can connect concepts across modalities. Explainable AI (XAI) techniques like Grad-CAM produce heatmaps highlighting important regions, and here Grad-ECLIP extends such methods to CLIP-based models for spatial localization.

## [TTT in VLA Models Fails to Improve Demonstration-Conditioned Action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32094) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper integrates Test-Time Training (TTT) with fast-weight memory into the InternVLA-A1 and Pi0.5 vision-language-action models to study action generation conditioned on human demonstrations. The authors find that at current scale, adding TTT and training on paired demonstrations and robot trajectories did not lead to consistent target selection based on demonstrations. This work provides a negative result for the robotics and VLA research community, suggesting that TTT and paired-data training may not be sufficient to achieve demonstration-conditioned behavior at current model scales. It highlights the need for alternative approaches or larger-scale experiments to enable robots to effectively follow human demonstrations in long-horizon tasks. The study implemented memory transfer between training phases and truncated backpropagation, and examined training objectives, gradient coverage, and contrastive regularization across video, short-video, and single-frame cue tasks. Even with shorter sequences, full trajectory gradient connectivity, and contrastive regularization, responsiveness to demonstrations did not clearly improve, and real-robot execution still preferred cups at fixed positions when demonstrations were reduced to single-frame cues.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Test-Time Training (TTT) is a technique where a model updates its weights (often called fast weights) during inference to adapt to each test instance, storing context in these fast weights. Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models combine visual perception, language understanding, and action generation to control robots, and are typically trained via imitation learning from human demonstrations. This paper explores whether TTT can help VLA models better retain and use past demonstration information for long-horizon tasks.

## [ReMedI: Inference-Time Refinement and Self-Distillation for Molecular Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32103) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ReMedI introduces an inference-time alignment approach that uses a pre-trained molecular language model as a proposer, combined with Metropolis-Hastings search to refine sequential edits for multi-objective lead optimization, and then distills the resulting search traces back into the base model. On the C-MuMOInstruct and SMDD benchmarks, it achieves about 2.8 times the strict-success coverage of the RL baseline C-MORAL while using roughly 7.8 times fewer training molecular leads. This work shows that costly task-specific RL fine-tuning may not be necessary for multi-objective molecular optimization, since a frozen pre-trained model can be guided effectively at inference time. It could lower the data and compute barriers for AI-driven drug discovery and make controllable molecular editing more accessible to smaller research groups. The method conditions edits on the current molecule and uses Metropolis-Hastings refinement to balance property improvement, preservation, and lead similarity, with improvements demonstrated across three backbone models. The distillation step also improves zero-shot generation and subsequent search, and gains extend to out-of-distribution tasks with matched lead-relative similarity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Lead optimization in drug development requires improving multiple competing properties, such as permeability and toxicity, while keeping the molecular scaffold stable enough to preserve potency. Molecular language models can propose edits, but zero-shot generation explores the space poorly, and RL-based post-training like C-MORAL requires expensive task-specific datasets. Metropolis-Hastings is a Markov chain Monte Carlo method that proposes new samples from a previous sample and accepts or rejects them based on a target distribution, making it well suited to guiding sequential molecular edits.

## [Neural Framework Solves Variational Problems on Graphons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32147) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a neural exploratory framework that represents graphons as implicit neural networks and optimizes graphon objectives via gradient descent, combining a multi-scale sinusoidal residual architecture, an embedded constraint solver, and symmetry-aware Monte Carlo estimators. The framework rediscovers known optimal graphons for generalized Turán problems without human intervention and finds new candidate optima for open instances and large deviation variational problems, including improvements over the Lubetzky–Zhao construction for the triangle graph upper-tail case. This work bridges extremal graph theory and large deviation theory with implicit neural representations, showing that neural optimization can autonomously discover extremal graph structures and even lead to new mathematical theorems. It could accelerate research in combinatorics and probability by providing a computational tool for exploring complex graphon optimization problems. The architecture is biased toward sharp, step-like graphons via multi-scale sinusoidal residual networks, and it enforces a single empirical density constraint using implicit differentiation. The symmetry-aware Monte Carlo estimators exploit graphon symmetries to improve optimization efficiency, and the framework has already produced candidate optima that led to new theorems on optimal graphons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Graphons are symmetric measurable functions from [0,1]^2 to [0,1] that arise as limits of dense graph sequences, providing a continuous representation of large graphs. Implicit neural representations (INRs) are neural networks that parameterize continuous signals directly as weights of a multilayer perceptron, making them discretization-independent and easily differentiable. Variational problems over graphons involve optimizing graphon objectives under constraints, and they appear in extremal graph theory (e.g., Turán-type problems) and in the large deviation theory of dense random graphs, where rare events are characterized by constrained graphon optimization.

## [MG-REPA aligns multi-granularity representations to speed up generative target speaker extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32161) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MG-REPA proposes aligning hidden states of a generative target speaker extraction model with a self-supervised learning model at the frame level and a speaker verification model at the utterance level, plus temporal normalization on the frame-level target. On Libri2Mix, it reaches the OVRL and PESQ of a MeanFlow-TSE baseline with 3.0x and 2.1x fewer epochs, respectively, and at 750 epochs is competitive with the published 2,000-epoch baseline on intrusive metrics while improving non-intrusive metrics. Generative TSE models produce high-quality extraction but converge slowly, so this multi-granularity alignment could substantially cut training cost and make diffusion-based speech separation more practical. It also shows that combining frame-level and utterance-level teachers outperforms single-granularity alignment, which may inform other generative speech tasks. The method uses an SSL teacher for time-varying content at the frame level and a speaker verification model for time-invariant speaker characteristics at the utterance level, with temporal normalization removing utterance-level statistics from the frame-level target. An ablation confirms MG-REPA outperforms alignment at a single granularity, though results are reported only on Libri2Mix and compared against a MeanFlow-TSE baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Target speaker extraction (TSE) isolates a desired speaker's voice from a mixed audio recording using an enrollment utterance or other reference cue. Generative TSE models, such as diffusion-based ones, can produce high-quality output but require many training epochs to converge. Representation alignment (REPA) was originally used to accelerate diffusion transformer training by aligning hidden states with a pre-trained self-supervised learning (SSL) teacher, but existing speech-domain versions align at only one granularity.

## [PPO-DAZ3: Adaptive Clipping via Action Confidence and Normalized Advantage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32194) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Adaptive Z-Score PPO (PPO-DAZ3), which replaces PPO's fixed clipping threshold with sample-dependent thresholds computed from action confidence (probability of the selected action) and update confidence (normalized advantage magnitude). It is evaluated on LunarLander-v3, CartPole-v1, and Acrobot-v1, showing gains mainly on the harder LunarLander-v3 task. Adaptive clipping could make PPO more sample-efficient and stable by allocating trust-region width according to update reliability, which matters for practitioners tuning PPO on new tasks. However, the limited evaluation on three classic control benchmarks means the impact is currently moderate and requires validation on more complex domains. PPO-DAZ3 leaves the rest of the standard PPO pipeline unchanged, and a smooth advantage-based variant, PPO-AZ3S, performs best on Acrobot-v1 while standard PPO remains competitive on CartPole-v1. The abstract is truncated, so full experimental details and hyperparameters are not available in the summary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) is a popular on-policy reinforcement learning algorithm that stabilizes training by clipping the policy update to a trust region, preventing large destructive changes. The clipping threshold is typically a fixed hyperparameter, meaning every sample gets the same allowed update magnitude regardless of how reliable that update is. Adaptive clipping variants aim to adjust this threshold dynamically based on signals like policy entropy or advantage estimates.

## [SOPO: Socratic-Guided RL for Span-Level Hallucination Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32212) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SOPO introduces a multi-turn reinforcement learning framework that uses a teacher model to provide Socratic hints during rollout, mitigating reward sparsity and localization ambiguity in span-level hallucination detection. The 4B model achieves state-of-the-art performance on RAGTruth, surpassing GPT-5 and standard RL baselines, with code and models released. This work shows that RL with verifiable rewards can be effectively extended to span-level hallucination detection, a finer-grained and harder task than sequence-level detection. It could improve the reliability of LLM outputs in RAG systems and other applications where precise localization of unfaithful content is critical. SOPO incorporates Turn-based Advantage Scaling to penalize verbosity bias, encouraging efficient reasoning. Empirical results show mixed evidence against CAPO on out-of-domain benchmarks, and the method is evaluated primarily on RAGTruth with 1.7B and 4B model sizes.

Span-level hallucination detection aims to identify the exact text spans that are hallucinated, which is more precise than sequence-level detection. Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) uses rule-based, programmatically verifiable rewards to train models, and has shown success in reasoning tasks like math and coding. Socratic feedback is a teaching method that uses indirect questioning to guide learners toward discovering answers themselves.

## [Easy-3Dfy decouples object generation from layout for single-image 3D scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32250) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Easy-3Dfy introduces a compositional workflow that separates 3D object generation from scene layout estimation, using an RPF-based aligner to establish geometric correspondence between object-local meshes and partial scene pointmaps. It consistently outperforms SAM-3Dfy on multiple geometric metrics on the Scene-Maker and 3D-FUTURE200 benchmarks without requiring complete scene-level supervision. This work shows that explicit geometric composition can combine high-quality object generation with strong spatial understanding from 3D foundation models, allowing the pipeline to benefit independently from advances in both areas. It offers a promising direction for improving fine-grained geometry and asset quality in single-image 3D scene reconstruction. The RPF-based aligner recovers object layout by matching object-local meshes to partial scene pointmaps, avoiding the need for complete scene-level supervision. The method is evaluated on Scene-Maker and 3D-FUTURE200, where it outperforms SAM-3Dfy across multiple geometric metrics.

<a id="item-282"></a>
## [Absolute Fix and Anti-coherence Regularization Improve SCALE for OOD Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32255) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces an Absolute Fix that computes SCALE's percentile-based magnitude ratio over absolute-valued activations instead of discarding negative activations via ReLU, correcting SCALE's degeneration on backbones like ViT. It further proposes Anti-coherence regularization, which penalizes the scaling factor based on the fraction of sign-mismatched max-logit contributions, suppressing unreliable evidence for the predicted class without requiring training data or labels. This work fixes a specific flaw in a popular post-hoc OOD detection method, enabling reliable OOD detection on modern architectures like ViT without any training data. It ties or outperforms training-data-dependent activation-shaping methods on CIFAR-10 and ImageNet under OpenOOD v1.5, which could make OOD detection more practical for deployed models where training data is unavailable. The Absolute Fix avoids the cancellation between positive and negative contributions in the underlying sum by using absolute values, while Anti-coherence regularization uses the sign-mismatch fraction of max-logit contributions to adjust the scaling factor. The method requires no access to training data or labels and is evaluated under the OpenOOD v1.5 protocol on CIFAR-10 and ImageNet, showing improvements in AUROC and FPR@95.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection aims to identify inputs that differ from the training distribution, which is crucial for AI safety. SCALE is a post-hoc activation-shaping method that rescales penultimate activations using a percentile-based magnitude ratio without training data. Activation shaping prunes and scales network activations before estimating the OOD energy score, achieving strong OOD detection with minimal in-distribution accuracy drop. However, SCALE's ratio is ill-defined when negative activations are present because positive and negative contributions cancel in the sum, causing degeneration on architectures like Vision Transformers (ViT).

## [CSFM: Composite Subspace Flow Matching for Self-Supervised Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32256) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes composite subspace flow matching (CSFM), a self-supervised representation learning method that replaces unstructured noise with a deterministic degradation curriculum across spectral, color, and illumination subspaces. By bounding trajectory variance with hard temporal style truncation, CSFM trains a capacity-constrained encoder to ignore transient style artifacts and encode persistent content. CSFM bridges the gap between joint-embedding architectures, which discard spatial details, and generative models, which struggle to isolate semantics, by steering the physical dynamics of generative flows. This offers a scalable paradigm for learning representations that are both linearly separable and generatively invertible, potentially impacting computer vision tasks like classification and synthesis. Empirical evaluations on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and STL-10 show that CSFM yields competitive discriminative transferability while preserving generative synthesis across both convolutional and vision transformer architectures. It outperforms standard optimal transport flows on linear probing and few-shot classification, while enabling coherent synthesis and trajectory interpolation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) trains models using data-generated supervisory signals instead of labels. Joint-embedding architectures, such as I-JEPA, predict representations of target blocks from a context block but may discard spatial details. Flow matching is a generative modeling approach that learns to transform noise into data via ordinary differential equations, and CSFM adapts it by using structured degradations rather than noise.

## [ChoreoStudio Plans Formations and Dancer Assignments for Group Dance Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32325) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ChoreoStudio introduces an explicit choreography planning framework that decomposes music into frame-level rhythmic and segment-level structural features, then uses a frozen omni-modal model as a scriptwriter to guide a Director module in deciding formation targets, transition timing, and dancer assignment. Experiments on AIOZ-GDance show state-of-the-art motion quality, group organization, and formation dynamics. This work moves beyond synchronized motion generation by explicitly planning what formation to form, when to change it, and who executes each transition, aligning group dance with high-level musical structure. It could benefit animation, virtual performance, and human-AI creative collaboration by making group choreography more controllable and semantically coherent. The Director produces three coupled decisions: a centroid-relative point set for formation, a transition gate for timing, and a mobility allocation for dancer assignment, with the latter two conditioned on the planned geometric change. A diffusion generator conditioned on frame-aligned music features handles detailed body motion, while the segment-level plan is injected via instruction tokens and cross-dancer attention biases.

## [TENET reframes multivariate time series anomaly detection as latent-space compatibility estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32337) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.32337) proposes TENET, a Time-point Encoding Network that reframes unsupervised multivariate time series anomaly detection (MTSAD) as current-point system-state compatibility estimation in latent space rather than observation-space reconstruction or forecasting error. TENET builds a latent representation of the target time point from variable-level temporal context using current-point-centered time embeddings and adaptive mixing, and scores it with a fixed standard-normal negative log-likelihood; it reports state-of-the-art results across benchmark datasets. Observation-space reconstruction and forecasting errors are easily inflated by local variation, making them unreliable anomaly indicators; moving the decision to latent space could yield more robust detectors for industrial monitoring, IT operations, and sensor networks. The paper also argues the gain comes from decision-aligned representation construction rather than a special scoring rule, which may guide how future one-class MTSAD models are designed. TENET treats the input window not as an object to reconstruct, forecast, summarize, or score, but as context for constructing a latent representation of the current point, which is then evaluated with a fixed standard-normal negative log-likelihood. Additional experiments show it remains effective under alternative one-class objectives and outperforms substitutions with generic sequence encoders, though the abstract gives no specific benchmark numbers or dataset names.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multivariate time series anomaly detection deals with data where many variables are observed simultaneously over time, and unsupervised or one-class settings assume training data contains mostly normal behavior. Many popular methods learn to reconstruct or forecast the input window and flag points with large errors, but local fluctuations can inflate those errors and cause false alarms. Latent-space scoring instead compares compact representations, and TENET's key idea is to center that representation on the current time point so the score directly reflects whether the system state at that point is compatible with normal behavior.

## [PASTA: Training-Free Diffusion Sampling Preserves Reserved Regions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32390) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PASTA introduces a training-free diffusion sampling framework that enforces reserved-region constraints by selectively applying semantic guidance only to object-allowed regions, using a shared latent trajectory and an annealed schedule to suppress semantic leakage early and restore consistency later. This approach improves layout-aware text-to-image generation by strictly preserving clean regions for headlines, logos, or UI elements without sacrificing overall image realism, which could streamline visual design workflows that require precise region control. PASTA fuses full-prompt and context-prompt guidance through mask-guided sampling on a shared latent trajectory, and its annealed schedule balances reserved-region fidelity with perceptual consistency, though the abstract does not specify quantitative results or comparisons.

## [Self-RefineVQA: Training-Free Self-Correction for Table VQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32423) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Self-RefineVQA, a training-free, inference-time self-correction framework in which a single vision-language model iteratively acts as generator, critic, and refiner to improve table visual question answering. Experiments on tabular VQA benchmarks show consistent accuracy gains in monolingual and cross-lingual settings, with especially large boosts for small 2B-7B parameter models. The approach avoids expensive supervised fine-tuning and expert-annotated data, making reliable table reasoning more accessible to resource-constrained teams. It also suggests that lightweight models can rival much larger counterparts through prompt-based self-improvement, which could reshape how document-image QA systems are deployed. The framework forms a closed self-feedback loop through prompt-guided re-evaluation, correcting spatial misalignment and logical hallucinations without model updates. Complexity analysis shows inference cost grows linearly, but the authors argue this is offset by superior data efficiency and lower cumulative cost compared with human labeling for fine-tuning.

## [DIGOM: Disentangled Interaction Graphs for Multimodal Healthcare Outcome Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32451) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DIGOM is a graph-based framework that jointly models three complementary interaction types—intra-patient cross-modal, cross-patient same-modal, and cross-patient cross-modal—using a sparse multiplex graph with disentangled views. It samples discrete adjacency matrices via a differentiable mechanism and achieves the highest AUROC on all five clinical prediction tasks across MIMIC-IV and NIH-TB datasets while using fewer connections than existing multi-interaction approaches. This work addresses a key gap in multimodal healthcare AI by explicitly modeling relational information that extends beyond individual patient observations, which standard fusion approaches overlook. The framework's ability to adapt relational structures per task and its robustness to missing modalities could improve clinical decision support in real-world settings where data are often incomplete. DIGOM learns a sparse multiplex graph with patient-adaptive connectivity, using a differentiable sampling mechanism that avoids imposing a fixed number of neighbors, and a regularization objective maintains overall sparsity. Ablation studies show that each interaction type's contribution varies across tasks, and post-hoc analyses reveal task- and outcome-specific connectivity patterns.

Standard fusion methods integrate modalities per patient but do not explicitly capture cross-patient or cross-modal interactions, while existing graph-based approaches typically model only a subset of these relationships. DIGOM builds on this by constructing a multiplex graph with disentangled views to capture all three interaction types simultaneously.

## [StarRTT: Real-Time Blind-Zone-Free RTT Prediction for Starlink](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32540) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers developed StarRTT, an end-to-end real-time framework that integrates per-second serving-satellite identification with RTT prediction to eliminate the identification blind zone in Starlink LEO networks. Its RT-LSTM model achieves a mean absolute error of 1.405 ms across 15 prediction horizons, reducing error by 4.3%, 11.6%, and 22.1% compared with LSTM, StarNet, and ASTM baselines, respectively. Accurate RTT prediction is critical for mitigating latency fluctuations caused by frequent satellite handovers in mega-constellations like Starlink, and eliminating the identification blind zone enables truly real-time prediction that could improve routing, congestion control, and application performance for satellite broadband users. This work advances the state of the art in LEO network measurement and prediction, benefiting researchers and operators focused on satellite networking. RT-LSTM employs a historical LSTM to encode past RTT observations and real-time serving-satellite and gateway-relative features, while a future LSTM processes TLE-propagated future features of the identified serving satellite up to the next scheduling instant. Under strict real-time back-testing, the reduction over LSTM reaches 12.5% at the scheduling instant, highlighting the benefit of real-time serving-satellite information when recent RTT observations are limited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Low Earth Orbit (LEO) satellite mega-constellations such as Starlink require frequent handovers due to high orbital velocities, causing substantial Round-Trip Time (RTT) fluctuations. Accurate RTT prediction can mitigate these fluctuations, but it relies on continuously updated serving-satellite information. Existing identification methods complete identification only at the end of each scheduling cycle, creating an identification blind zone that limits real-time prediction. StarRTT addresses this by integrating per-second serving-satellite identification with RTT prediction using an LSTM-based model.

## [EditJev: Single-Pass Routing for Image Editing Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32635) ⭐️ 6.0/10

EditJev introduces a System One routing model that predicts sample-level preference scores over candidate image editing models in a single forward pass, using a lightweight preference head on a vision-language backbone. Experiments on ImgEdit and GEdit-En show quality improvements over the strongest fixed editor (4.540 to 4.592 and 8.194 to 8.255) while reducing estimated end-to-end latency by 60.8% and 31.7%. Fixed-model deployment is inefficient because instruction-based image editing models vary widely in quality and latency across samples. EditJev's direct, structured routing could enable more efficient and higher-quality editing services by dynamically selecting the best editor per request. The preference head produces a fixed-dimensional score vector without autoregressive model-name generation or response parsing, and training uses pairwise preferences from offline candidate outputs with a pairwise ranking objective. At inference, predicted preference scores are combined with model-level speed scores to balance quality and latency.

'System One' refers to fast, intuitive decision-making, contrasting with slower deliberate reasoning, and here denotes a single-forward-pass routing approach.

## [AI Agents Fall Short in Industrial Robot Programming, Case Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32636) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A case study with a small and medium-sized enterprise (SME) that programs industrial robots found that AI agents did not improve engineer productivity, except for a task requiring highly repetitive code generation. However, results improved over time as engineers learned to use the agent and the agent was adapted to the domain. This empirical evidence challenges the assumption that AI agents are plug-and-play solutions and highlights that AI experience matters more than domain experience for success with agents. A new arXiv paper evaluates Conditional Neural Processes (CNPs) with an added attention mechanism in the hidden space for autonomously learning action representations that are informative about their underlying goal in robotic imitation learning. The authors show that this attention addition fosters the emergence of robust goal representations early in the observation of an action sequence. This work suggests a promising model for a robotic self-supervised general learner, potentially reducing the need for explicit goal labels and enabling robots to imitate and generalize from limited demonstrations. It connects biologically inspired imitation learning with probabilistic meta-learning, which could benefit robotics researchers working on sample-efficient skill acquisition. Attention mechanisms allow neural networks to dynamically weight different parts of the input, focusing on the most relevant information. The paper introduces an agentic, ontology-guided framework that uses specialized LLM agents to extract evidence-backed triples from climate science papers and verify them against an ontology, producing a knowledge graph with 133,990 nodes and 755,308 relations from 2,000 publications. In an agreement study over 1,000 triplets, Gemini-2.5-flash achieved Cohen's kappa scores of 0.640–0.700 against three human annotators, matching human–human agreement (0.616–0.689), and 98.96% of the 23,327 validated triplets were judged correct. ## [PlayerEmbed Learns Behavioral Fingerprints from Raw Game Input Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32727) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PlayerEmbed introduces a hybrid CNN-LSTM encoder trained with deep metric learning on raw input sequences from 1,000 players to produce compact player embeddings. Using a nearest-centroid classifier, it achieves balanced accuracies of 0.950 for seen players and 0.879 for 1,000 entirely unseen players. This work shows that raw, noisy input sequences can yield transferable behavioral representations, enabling player identification and large-scale game analytics without handcrafted features. It could benefit game studios, anti-cheat systems, and player modeling research. The model is evaluated on held-out sequences from training players and on sequences from 1,000 unseen players, with generalization improving substantially as the number of training players increases. ## [Clean-label poisoning attack and defense game for federated learning in vehicular networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32765) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a feature-space clean-label poisoning attack that perturbs benign samples toward malicious feature extremes while preserving labels, and proposes a feature-consistency relabeling defense to detect and relabel suspicious samples. A game-theoretic framework is developed to characterize equilibrium strategies based on attack effectiveness and cost parameters. This work addresses a critical security threat in federated learning for vehicular networks, where compromised nodes can degrade anomaly detection without altering labels. The proposed defense maintains clean accuracy while shifting equilibrium toward attack-averse regimes, which could inform robust FL deployment in safety-critical vehicular systems. The attack scales effectively with the number of compromised nodes, and the defense keeps attack success low without degrading clean accuracy. The analysis models Basic Safety Message classification for anomaly detection as a distributed learning problem over a dynamic-topology vehicular network.

It finds that removing gender from voter profiles reduces the largest group error by 22–42% and removing education reduces it by 64%, while removing self-reported race increases mean group error by over 40%, all without lowering overall accuracy. PoKER introduces a machine-learning probabilistic model to estimate air-to-air missile kill probability in beyond-visual-range (BVR) combat, accounting for stochastic target behavior and missile miss distances. Unlike conventional Weapon Engagement Zone (WEZ) models that rely on static distances, PoKER dynamically evaluates target behavior and incorporates factors such as warhead lethality, target and shooter orientations, and engagement conditions. ## [Yalda-Embedding: Open Persian Retrieval Model Fine-Tuned from Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32933) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Yalda Embedding is a 595.8M-parameter Persian dense retrieval model fine-tuned from Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B on over one million query-document pairs. On eight Persian retrieval tasks from FaMTEB, it achieves the highest macro-average (0.5488) among evaluated 0.6B models, outperforming Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B and F2LLM-v2-0.6B on every task, and approaches Qwen3-Embedding-4B (winning on four tasks, tying on one, and trailing by only 0.0075 in macro-average). This work demonstrates that a compact 0.6B model can be efficiently adapted to a lower-resource language like Persian and rival much larger models, making high-quality retrieval more accessible. The full release of weights, code, and datasets promotes open development and reproducibility for Persian NLP, which has lagged behind high-resource languages in embedding resources. An incremental ablation study shows that contrastive fine-tuning alone yields only modest gains, while relational knowledge distillation from F2LLM-v2-8B improves all eight tasks, raising the mean from 0.4813 to 0.5488 without increasing inference-time model size. Notably, gains include hard-negative evaluation tasks even though training did not use explicitly mined hard negatives.

Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a larger teacher model (here F2LLM-v2-8B) to a smaller student model, and relational distillation specifically matches the relationships between embeddings rather than individual outputs.

A new arXiv paper (2610.32945) presents a theoretical model of a monopolistic LLM platform that jointly sets response quality, advertising intensity, and subscription prices. It finds that ad-supported tiers can broaden access while polarizing user utility, and that an intermediate ad cap may maximize consumer surplus. The model assumes advertising reduces user value while better service raises advertising returns, and shows that cheaper compute can raise quality and lower ad intensity yet, if cost reductions are large enough, increase total ad revenue above its initial level. ## [Expressivity Results for Structured Argumentation with Uncertain Rules and Premises](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18631) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a comparative expressivity notion for abstract and structured argumentation frameworks under qualitative uncertainty, proving both positive and negative results. It compares incomplete abstract argumentation frameworks and their extension with dependencies against structured formalisms ASPIC+, assumption-based argumentation (ABA), and deductive argumentation. This work addresses an open theoretical question about whether abstract models of argumentation under uncertainty can be plausibly instantiated by structured models, which is important for both practical applications and theoretical understanding. It provides a formal basis for comparing the expressive power of different argumentation formalisms, potentially guiding future research in knowledge representation. The comparative expressivity notion is defined such that one formalism is at least as expressive as another if it can generate the same set of abstract argumentation frameworks (completions). The results cover incomplete abstract argumentation frameworks and their extension with dependencies on the abstract side, and ASPIC+, ABA, and deductive argumentation on the structured side.

It introduces Transformer-based architectures for predictive maintenance, scalable sample- and population-level causal discovery frameworks, and a multi-agent system that synthesizes Boolean error pattern rules, replacing manual rule-based grouping. The thesis notes that a modern vehicle contains tens of thousands of unique DTCs, comparable in magnitude to a natural language vocabulary, and that traditional statistical methods fail to capture rich dependencies or scale to datasets with thousands of nodes, large sample sizes, and long sequences. A new arXiv preprint presents a reproducible spatial simulation audit that combines a generative adversarial network (GAN) for patrol allocation with a probabilistic detection model, using frozen archives of 145,178 Baltimore records (2017–2019) and 234,882 Chicago records (2022). The audit uses 20 random seeds per condition to generate 560 trajectories covering February through December, and it finds that a conditional tabular generative model for balanced synthetic replacement fails geographic consistency requirements in 107 of 220 seed-month fits, leaving no complete annual synthetic-replacement trajectories. The authors caution that results do not estimate individual racial disparities in police contact or the distribution of unrecorded crime.

The paper introduces a Latent Diagnostic Trajectory Learning (LDTL) framework that uses a planning LLM agent and a diagnostic LLM agent to sequentially acquire clinical evidence under uncertainty. Experiments on the MIMIC-CDM benchmark show that LDTL outperforms existing baselines in diagnostic accuracy while requiring fewer diagnostic tests. Most LLM-based diagnostic systems assume fully observed patient information and do not model how clinical evidence should be sequentially acquired over time. The framework treats diagnostic action sequences as latent paths and introduces a posterior distribution that prioritizes trajectories providing more diagnostic information; the planning LLM agent is trained to follow this distribution. NORA proposes a noise-robust tagging method for rich financial numerical entity attributes, using task-aware instance-specific weighting to attenuate noisy labels during training. It also introduces an evaluation protocol that filters noisy labels in real-world test sets and constructs a large-scale benchmark of 11.4 million entities in 6.3 million sentences with multi-attribute labels and filing metadata. This work addresses a key bottleneck in financial NLP: labels derived from inline XBRL filings are often noisy due to manual preparation, and existing studies focus mainly on concept name prediction while neglecting other important attributes. By modeling rich attributes with document context and accounting for label noise, NORA could improve the reliability of automated financial statement analysis and benefit researchers and practitioners working with regulatory filings. NORA achieves the best mean Accuracy and Macro F1 over four attributes under both unfiltered and noise-filtered test settings, with the largest gain on time-relation prediction while remaining competitive on concept name, scale, and sign. ## [SCENE: Bi-level multi-agent framework contextualizes biomedical knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27082) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces SCENE, a bi-level multi-agent framework that transforms broad biomedical knowledge into evidence-supported, scenario-grounded propositions through iterative search and multi-objective optimization. It was evaluated on clinical trial subgroup discovery and LINCS L1000 perturbational contexts, outperforming baselines in the former and achieving strong target-response matching in the latter. This work addresses the gap between general biomedical knowledge and specific experimental data, potentially enabling more interpretable and traceable hypothesis generation for clinical and biological research. It could benefit researchers in drug discovery and personalized medicine by providing inspectable, evidence-backed propositions. SCENE uses a bi-level architecture: the upper level converts broad knowledge into search directions grounded in dataset schema, while the lower level executes these directions via multi-objective optimization to balance evidential strength and data support. Feedback between levels refines the search, and the framework was tested on clinical trial subgroups and LINCS L1000 studies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Biomedical discovery often requires linking broad background knowledge with specific experimental or clinical data, but general knowledge is too vague to map directly to dataset variables, and data-driven patterns are hard to interpret mechanistically. Knowledge contextualization aims to bridge this gap by transforming broad knowledge into concrete, evidence-supported propositions that experts can validate. Multi-agent systems and multi-objective optimization are computational approaches that can automate this process by dividing tasks among agents and optimizing trade-offs between conflicting objectives.

## [Quality-Aware Self-Distillation Boosts GUI Grounding Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18101) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a quality-aware self-distillation method for GUI grounding that uses a soft gate to verify teacher coordinate predictions and down-weight unreliable signals when student-generated prefixes deviate from the target. It also applies teacher-probability scaling to calibrate the gated supervision, and experiments across six benchmarks show consistent improvements over the base model and strong baselines. GUI grounding is critical for vision-language models to interact with screens, and this work addresses a key flaw in on-policy self-distillation where teacher signals become unreliable under student-generated prefixes. The finding that gating and probability scaling are complementary could inform future post-training methods for coordinate-sensitive tasks. A key empirical finding is that neither the soft gate nor teacher-probability scaling alone improves overall performance, but combining them consistently does, suggesting complementary roles. The method is evaluated on six GUI grounding benchmarks and outperforms strong baselines.

## [DeFAb: A Dataset for Grounded Scientific Discovery via Defeasible Abduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18557) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DeFAb is a new dataset and symbolic verification framework built from curated Gene Ontology knowledge, where models fill gaps in a theory or propose exceptions to its rules, and a verifier checks the consequences of each edit. Simple symbolic repairs solve all 336 tasks in the released evaluation panel, exposing a limitation of the revision test and motivating a contextual task that requires exceptions to generalize to relevant cases while preserving predictions elsewhere. This work highlights that simple exception-based theory revision can succeed without identifying generalizable patterns, which is a key challenge for AI systems performing scientific reasoning. The dataset and verifier provide a controlled testbed for studying defeasible abduction, potentially advancing automated scientific discovery and robust reasoning under uncertainty. The framework allows different solutions to receive credit by checking consequences rather than exact matches, and two planned studies will compare training with verified versus rejected examples and revision with and without feedback about consequences. The contextual task specifically tests whether an exception reaches the relevant cases and preserves predictions elsewhere, addressing the limitation of entity-specific exceptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Defeasible reasoning allows conclusions to be withdrawn when new information contradicts them, and abduction is the process of inferring the best explanation for observations. The Gene Ontology is a major bioinformatics resource providing a controlled vocabulary of gene and gene product attributes across species, which serves as the curated biological knowledge base for DeFAb. Symbolic verification uses formal methods to check whether a proposed solution satisfies given constraints, enabling automated evaluation of theory revisions.

## [Attractor Domain Theory: A New Math Framework for Cardiovascular Analysis from Wearable PPG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22039) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Attractor Domain Theory (ADT), a mathematical framework that uses delay-embedding and domain transformations to analyze cardiovascular dynamics from wearable photoplethysmography (PPG) data. It defines three representations—Geometry, Ergodic, and Variational domains—and validates the approach on 176,742 PPG segments (AUC 0.757) and 5,000 ECG records (AUC 0.719). This framework offers a principled way to extract clinically relevant information from widely available wearable PPG sensors, potentially enabling non-invasive cardiovascular monitoring outside clinical settings. It bridges nonlinear dynamics theory with practical health monitoring, which could impact both wearable device developers and clinicians. The framework includes a Conditional Domain Decomposition with an explicit residual and a domain-transformation impossibility theorem showing that properties cannot be exactly recovered when an operator maps distinct inputs to the same representation. Cross-observation transfer is property-specific: the Lyapunov exponent shows subject-level ECG-PPG agreement (ρ=0.703), but the predicted class-wide separation of invariant and observation-coupled descriptors is not supported (p=0.54).

**Background**: Photoplethysmography (PPG) is an optical technique used in wearable sensors to detect blood volume changes, commonly for heart rate monitoring. Delay-embedding theorems, such as Takens' theorem, allow reconstruction of a system's phase-space dynamics from a single scalar time series, enabling the study of nonlinear cardiovascular dynamics. Attractor Domain Theory builds on these concepts to provide a structured mathematical framework for analyzing cardiac attractors from PPG signals.

A new arXiv paper (2607.20464v2) uses correlation-matrix eigenvalues to show that apparent cross-question error structure in LLM benchmarks largely stems from shared random seeds, few-shot prompts, and an answer-letter preference concentrated in Llama-3.2-1B, rather than from missing knowledge. This work cautions that benchmark evaluations may be confounded by methodological artifacts, potentially leading to incorrect inferences about model knowledge and unfair comparisons across models. The analysis across 24 models reveals that the largest structure beyond overall skill is a preference among answer letters that follows the letter when options are rotated, and this effect depends on Llama-3.2-1B; without it, only a marginal excess at the 5% significance line remains, tied post hoc to two near-duplicate model pairs.

Llama-3.2-1B is a small language model from Meta's Llama family, with 1 billion parameters.
