## [ID-TAP: Information-Directed Dueling Bandits for Test-Time LLM Agent Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24385) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present ID-TAP, an information-directed dueling-bandit framework that personalizes LLM agents at test time by selecting personas from binary comparisons between candidate responses. Across seven user-conditioned generation benchmarks, ID-TAP achieves the strongest aggregate performance among evaluated baselines within a fixed ten-round comparison budget. This work shows that lightweight binary feedback, rather than explicit preference labels or fine-tuning, can drive effective test-time adaptation of LLM agents, which could lower the interaction cost of personalization in deployed systems. It also connects dueling-bandit theory with neural utility modeling, offering a scalable approach for large discrete persona spaces. ID-TAP combines a query-conditioned neural utility model with a lightweight Bradley-Terry explorer, and transfers comparison evidence across related queries through similarity-weighted warm starts. The neural model shares statistical strength across queries while preserving query-specific scores, and efficient Bradley-Terry updates score hypothetical challengers.

**Background**: Test-time personalization requires an LLM agent to adapt to a user's preferences during interaction, without retraining the underlying model. Dueling bandits are a preference-learning framework where the learner only observes which of two options is preferred, rather than absolute scores. Information-directed sampling (IDS) chooses actions that maximize the ratio of information gained to regret, and the Bradley-Terry model converts pairwise comparisons into latent utility scores.

## [EASformer disentangles environment, action, and state for industrial generative ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27691) ⭐️ 6.0/10

EASformer introduces an Environment-Action-State disentangled architecture that explicitly aligns the context environment space shared by historical and non-sequential tokens while separating user actions from candidate-specific context. On an offline benchmark from a real-world industrial app marketplace, it achieves an AUC of 0.89411 (a 0.88% relative improvement over the deployed baseline), and online A/B tests show relative eCPM lifts of 1.26% and 3.94% across two ranking scenarios. This work addresses a real semantic asymmetry in generative recommendation systems, where query-key dot products between historical and candidate-side representations compare different semantic objects, potentially limiting cross-token interaction. The disentangled design and Cloze-style self-supervised objective offer a practical path for industrial ranking systems that jointly model long behavior sequences and heterogeneous features. The architecture constructs an aligned context environment space shared by historical and non-sequence tokens, disentangles user actions from historical tokens and candidate-specific context from non-sequence tokens, and supports complementary historical action retrieval and state-conditioned action prediction. The Cloze-style objective masks historical actions and reuses the candidate prediction pathway, though the reported gains are incremental and evaluated on a single industrial app marketplace.

**Background**: Generative recommendation (GR) paradigms tokenize user behavior sequences and non-sequential features and jointly model them with Transformer-style backbones, providing a scalable interface for long histories and heterogeneous features. In Transformer attention, query-key dot products measure compatibility between representations; when those representations encode different semantic objects, the comparison becomes less meaningful. EASformer targets this issue by explicitly aligning a shared environment space while disentangling action and state information.

## [RoSA Framework Tackles Saliency Collapse in Long-Text Image-Text Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31005) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper identifies a previously overlooked failure mode called 'saliency collapse' in long-text image-text alignment, where models over-rely on visually prominent text and underutilize non-salient context, causing retrieval performance to degrade sharply when salient content is reduced. To address this, the authors propose RoSA, a saliency-aware finetuning framework that decomposes image-text pairs into complementary salient and contextual views using a modality-independent strategy combining object-level visual saliency estimation with LLM-based text decomposition, and employs plug-and-play auxiliary objectives to align these views. This work highlights a critical robustness issue in vision-language models that could affect real-world retrieval systems where salient cues are often missing or ambiguous. The proposed plug-and-play auxiliary objectives can be integrated into existing tuning methods, potentially improving long-text retrieval across a wide range of multimodal applications. RoSA combines object-level visual saliency estimation with LLM-based text decomposition to create modality-independent salient and contextual views, and uses auxiliary objectives to explicitly align these views. The method is evaluated on long-text retrieval benchmarks, showing strong robustness to saliency collapse, and the auxiliary objectives bring consistent gains when added to existing tuning methods; code and checkpoints are available on GitHub.

## [Quantized Orthogonal RNNs Achieve 1000-Step Copy Task](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04012) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes two strategies to learn quantized approximately orthogonal RNNs (QORNNs) by combining quantization-aware training (QAT) with orthogonal projections, and also studies post-training quantization of activations for pure integer computation in the recurrent loop. The resulting fully-quantized models are the only alternatives capable of solving the Copy-task with 1000 timesteps and surpass existing quantized models on long-term dependency tasks like permuted MNIST and some IoT tasks. This work addresses a known open problem—the instability of quantizing orthogonal RNNs—and enables their deployment on compact devices by reducing precision. It demonstrates that fully-quantized ORNNs can handle extremely long-term dependencies (1000 steps), which is crucial for edge computing and IoT applications where memory and compute are limited. The authors combine QAT with orthogonal projections to maintain approximate orthogonality in quantized weights, and they also quantize activations post-training for integer-only inference. However, the quantized models are less effective on tasks where LSTMs and other gated RNNs outperform ORNNs, indicating a trade-off between efficiency and accuracy on certain workloads.

**Background**: Orthogonal Recurrent Neural Networks (ORNNs) use orthogonal weight matrices to mitigate the vanishing and exploding gradient problems in standard RNNs, enabling them to learn long-term dependencies with linear complexity. Quantization reduces the precision of weights and activations (e.g., to 8-bit integers), which shrinks model size and speeds up inference on hardware with limited resources. Quantization-aware training (QAT) simulates quantization during training so the model can adapt and maintain accuracy after deployment.

The survey finds that AI can enhance memory, creativity, and engagement but also brings risks like diminished critical thinking, skill erosion, and increased anxiety. The TRIAD model is recursive, linking appraisal (how users evaluate AI outputs), execution (how they act on them), and regulation (how they adjust their behavior), which captures dynamic feedback loops rather than static effects. ## [LAPS: Lightweight Adaptive Policy Switcher for Edge Process Scheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11628) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers have proposed LAPS, a framework that dynamically switches between predefined expert scheduling policies to adapt to mixed workloads on edge devices. It uses an offline preparation phase to precompute a Policy Mapping Table, and at runtime performs only lightweight workload classification and table lookups, achieving an average 8.91% performance gain and up to 91.7% reduction in response latency for latency-sensitive tasks compared to the default Linux EEVDF scheduler. This work addresses the limitations of static 'one-policy-fits-all' schedulers in dynamic edge environments without incurring the high overhead of AI-assisted policies, offering a practical path toward more responsive and intelligent operating systems. It could benefit edge device manufacturers and OS developers seeking to improve performance under diverse workloads. LAPS decouples high-frequency context switching from low-frequency policy transitions and amortizes transitional overheads by pre-resolving heavy dynamic tradeoffs offline. The runtime agentic Observe-Infer-Switch cycle limits machine learning inference strictly to workload classification, and the code is publicly available on GitHub.

Adaptive scheduling approaches exist, but static policies lack runtime adaptability, while AI-assisted policies introduce unacceptable overhead for resource-constrained devices. LAPS aims to balance these tradeoffs by combining offline preparation with lightweight runtime adaptation.

## [Training-Free Entropy Method Fixes Flow-Based OOD Likelihood Paradox](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09581) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Semantic Proportional Entropy Manipulation (SPEM), a training-free procedure that applies stronger perturbations to inputs less similar to an in-distribution memory bank, thereby mitigating the likelihood paradox in flow-based out-of-distribution (OOD) detection. Theoretical lower bounds show that this entropy gap increases the expected log-likelihood difference in favor of in-distribution data, and experiments confirm that the manipulation realigns likelihood ordering with human intuition. This work offers a simple, post-hoc fix to a well-known failure mode of normalizing flows, potentially improving the reliability of likelihood-based OOD detection without retraining models. It could benefit practitioners deploying generative models in safety-critical settings where distinguishing in-distribution from out-of-distribution inputs is crucial. The method is training-free and relies on an in-distribution memory bank to compute similarity, applying perturbations proportional to dissimilarity. Notably, the realignment persists even when the flow is given visually meaningless noise instead of the original input, and the authors provide a theoretical characterization of the conditions under which this occurs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Normalizing flows are deep generative models that can compute exact likelihoods for inputs, but they often assign unexpectedly high likelihoods to out-of-distribution (OOD) data—a phenomenon known as the likelihood paradox. This undermines their use for OOD detection, where the goal is to flag inputs that differ from the training distribution. Entropy-based methods manipulate uncertainty to improve separation between in-distribution and OOD data.

**Tags**: `#out-of-distribution detection`, `#normalizing flows`, `#entropy`, `#deep generative models`, `#machine learning`

<a id="item-325"></a>
## [Counterintuitive Likelihood Behavior Rare in Tabular Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09593) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that counterintuitive likelihood behavior—where anomalous data receives higher likelihoods than normal data—rarely occurs in tabular anomaly detection, unlike in image domains. The authors introduce a domain-agnostic formulation and conduct extensive experiments on 47 tabular datasets and 10 CV/NLP embedding datasets from ADBench, comparing against 13 baselines. This finding suggests that normalizing flows with likelihood-based scoring are a practical and effective approach for tabular anomaly detection, potentially simplifying model selection and deployment in tabular domains. It also highlights that insights from image anomaly detection may not directly transfer to tabular data, affecting practitioners and researchers working with tabular data. The paper provides theoretical and empirical analyses attributing the disparity to differences in dimensionality and feature correlation between tabular and image data. The experiments include 47 tabular datasets and 10 CV/NLP embedding datasets from ADBench, benchmarked against 13 baseline models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Normalizing flows are deep generative models that provide tractable likelihoods, making them suitable for anomaly detection by scoring data points based on their likelihood under a model trained on normal data. In image anomaly detection, deep generative models often assign higher likelihoods to anomalous data, a counterintuitive phenomenon that has been studied but lacked a precise definition. ADBench is a comprehensive benchmark for anomaly detection, covering numerous datasets and algorithms.

## [CrimeNER-db: A New Annotated Dataset for Crime-Related Named Entity Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02150) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The authors introduce CrimeNER-db, a dataset of over 1.5K annotated documents from public reports of terrorist attacks and US Department of Justice press notes, with 4 coarse and 21 fine-grained entity types. They benchmark the dataset using flat and nested NER architectures as well as zero-shot and few-shot experiments with large language models. This dataset fills a real gap in annotated data for crime-related information extraction, providing a valuable resource for law enforcement agencies and researchers. It also offers a benchmark for evaluating both traditional NER models and LLMs in a niche but important domain. The dataset is publicly available on GitHub and includes experiments with flat and nested NER models, as well as zero-shot and few-shot LLM evaluations. The 21 fine-grained entity types allow for detailed semantic capture, though the dataset size (1.5K documents) may limit deep learning approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Named Entity Recognition (NER) is the task of identifying and classifying entities (e.g., persons, organizations, locations) in text. Flat NER assumes entities do not overlap, while nested NER handles entities contained within others, capturing finer-grained information. Large language models (LLMs) can perform NER in zero-shot or few-shot settings, where they are given no or few examples, respectively. Crime-related documents often contain complex entity structures, making both flat and nested approaches relevant.

<a id="item-327"></a>
## [VORL-EXPLORE: Hybrid Learning-Planning for Multi-Robot Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07973) ⭐️ 6.0/10

VORL-EXPLORE introduces execution fidelity, an estimate of local navigability and execution reliability, to couple Voronoi-based frontier allocation with reinforcement learning-based navigation. The fidelity score penalizes risky frontiers in a fidelity-coupled Voronoi allocation objective and arbitrates between global A* guidance and a reactive RL policy during navigation. This work addresses a key brittleness in hierarchical multi-robot exploration: allocators that ignore execution difficulty can send robots into congested or unsafe routes, causing bottlenecks and redundant coverage. By making exploration execution-aware, the approach could improve success rates and efficiency in dynamic environments such as warehouses and factories. The fidelity model is recalibrated using self-supervised pseudo-labels derived from recent progress and safety outcomes, and experiments on randomized dynamic grids show higher success rates, shorter exploration lengths, and lower inter-robot coverage overlap. A Gazebo factory scenario is included as a deployment illustration, and the source code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-robot exploration typically uses a hierarchical architecture: a global allocator assigns frontier targets to robots, while a local planner or policy handles navigation to those targets. Voronoi diagrams are often used to partition space among robots for balanced allocation, and A* is a classic graph search algorithm for global path planning, while reinforcement learning can provide reactive local control. The novelty here is feeding an estimate of execution difficulty back into both allocation and navigation arbitration.

<a id="item-328"></a>
## [MetaKE: Bi-Level Meta-Learning Improves Accuracy-Editability Trade-off in Knowledge Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12677) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MetaKE is a new framework that unifies the upstream representation optimization and downstream constrained parameter optimization stages of locate-then-edit knowledge editing into a bi-level optimization problem. It introduces a Structural Gradient Proxy to approximate downstream feedback and avoid costly multi-layer backpropagation, and experiments show it outperforms strong baselines. This work addresses a key disconnect in existing locate-then-edit methods, where uniform regularization ignores downstream realizability, leading to suboptimal editing accuracy and editability. By enabling request-specific regularization through meta-learning, MetaKE offers a new perspective on improving knowledge editing performance, which is important for maintaining up-to-date and factual language models. The bi-level formulation treats parameter updates as the inner level and representation optimization as the outer level, using downstream constraint feedback to guide representation learning. The Structural Gradient Proxy approximates this feedback to avoid the computational cost of full multi-layer backpropagation, making the approach practical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge editing aims to update specific facts in large language models without retraining. Locate-then-edit methods first locate the parameters responsible for a fact and then modify them, but they often struggle to balance editing accuracy (successfully changing the fact) and editability (preserving unrelated knowledge). Meta-learning, or learning to learn, trains models on a distribution of tasks to improve generalization, and bi-level optimization is a framework for problems with nested optimization objectives.

## [Equity Bias: A Hermeneutic Framework for Transparent AI Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21907) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The arXiv paper 'Equity Bias' proposes a philosophical and practical framework that treats AI as an interpretive agent and makes bias transparent and contestable through a three-stage life cycle (Equity Archaeology, Co-Creating Meaning, and Ongoing Accountability), rather than attempting to eliminate bias. This framework challenges the dominant bias-elimination paradigm in AI ethics by centering the politics of knowledge and epistemic injustice, offering developers, policymakers, and affected communities a method to examine whose knowledge is encoded and who can contest it. Grounded in Gadamerian hermeneutics and epistemic injustice theory, the framework argues that since all knowledge is situated, neutral systems are impossible; its three-stage life cycle operationalizes principles of transparency and contestability, though the paper is conceptual and lacks empirical validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Gadamerian hermeneutics is a philosophical approach to interpretation emphasizing that understanding always occurs from a particular historical and cultural horizon. Epistemic injustice theory examines how people can be wronged in their capacity as knowers, for example when their testimony is unfairly discounted. Traditional AI bias mitigation often treats bias as a technical error to be reduced, but this paper reframes bias as an inevitable reflection of situated knowledge.

## [StemBind benchmark pinpoints rule-to-instance binding failure in multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00148) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced StemBind, a shared-stem diagnostic benchmark that decomposes abstract visual reasoning into perception, rule induction, and answer selection using three aligned questions per visual stem. Evaluating 24 frontier MLLM configurations revealed a persistent binding gap: even when models correctly perceive and identify the rule, they still answer the full item incorrectly 51.2% of the time. This work reframes abstract visual reasoning evaluation from final-answer ranking to locating exactly where reasoning breaks down, identifying rule-to-instance binding as a concrete target for improving vision-grounded reasoning. It shows that scaling model size or enabling thinking mode does not reliably close this gap, which has direct implications for how the community evaluates and designs multimodal reasoning systems. StemBind contains 2,298 curated knowledge-light stems across nine auditable visual operations, totaling 19,533 perception/rule/full tasks, with each full item annotated by Sternberg's four reasoning stages (Encode, Infer, Map, Apply). The analysis localizes the dominant failure to the Map stage (S3), and notably, explicit thinking mode even lowers rule and full-item accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Abstract visual reasoning (AVR) tasks require models to infer underlying patterns from visual stimuli and select the correct completion, similar to human IQ-test puzzles. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) often appear to understand these patterns yet fail to choose the right answer, but traditional benchmarks collapse perception, rule induction, and answer selection into a single right-or-wrong score, making it impossible to diagnose where the failure occurs. Sternberg's four-stage model of reasoning provides a theoretical framework for decomposing these sub-steps.

## [SteerVTE: Seamless Video Text Editing with Style and Glyph Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23254) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SteerVTE introduces a unified framework that steers a frozen video diffusion model with style and glyph control to perform precise, temporally coherent video text editing. The method attaches a lightweight text context adapter with a style encoder and dual-granularity glyph encoders, and introduces a glyph-aware spatial-focal loss and a three-stage progressive training curriculum, along with a new dataset SteerVTE-1M of one million triplets. Video text editing is largely unexplored compared to image text editing, and this work addresses the unique challenges of cross-frame accuracy, temporal coherence, and stylistic fidelity. The proposed approach could enable practical applications in video post-production, advertising, and content localization, where modifying text in videos is currently labor-intensive. SteerVTE operates on a frozen diffusion transformer backbone, using a style encoder to capture the original text's visual attributes and dual-granularity glyph encoders to encode target text at line and character levels. The glyph-aware spatial-focal loss and three-stage progressive training curriculum (scaling from image to video data) help overcome weak text rendering priors in video foundation models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise data by reversing a gradual noise-adding process, and diffusion transformers (DiTs) replace the traditional U-Net backbone with a transformer architecture, improving scalability and performance. Video text editing requires modifying text within video frames while maintaining visual realism and consistency across frames, a task complicated by the small size of text regions and the need for stroke-level precision.

## [Prediction-Powered Data Fusion for Treatment Effect Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12332) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a prediction-powered data fusion framework that combines small randomized controlled trials (RCTs) with large observational studies (OBS) to improve the precision of average and conditional treatment effect estimates while preserving RCT unbiasedness. It proposes an ATE estimator, AIPW-Fusion, with closed-form weights and confidence intervals, and two CATE learners, DR-Fusion and R-Fusion. This work addresses a key challenge in causal inference: how to leverage large but confounded observational data to enhance the precision of small RCTs without introducing bias. If effective, it could enable more efficient and generalizable treatment effect estimation in fields like medicine and policy evaluation, where RCTs are often limited in size. The framework avoids special assumptions on the observational study, unlike existing methods that either assume unconfoundedness or model the confounding function. The ATE estimator has closed-form weights and confidence intervals, and the CATE learners are based on doubly robust and regression-based fusion approaches; experiments corroborate the theoretical findings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Randomized controlled trials (RCTs) are the gold standard for estimating causal effects because randomization eliminates confounding, but they are often small and expensive. Observational studies (OBS) are larger and cheaper but subject to confounding, where treatment assignment is related to potential outcomes. Data fusion methods aim to combine these sources to improve precision while maintaining validity. The average treatment effect (ATE) and conditional average treatment effect (CATE) are common estimands; the latter captures heterogeneity across subgroups.

## [Finetuning neural networks for structured latent spaces speeds up similarity search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29295) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes finetuning ArcFace-pretrained IResNet50 models using Latent Space Configuration (LSC) to create structured latent spaces that enable O(1) candidate subset search, yielding up to 3x GPU and 40% CPU speedups for top-K similarity retrieval compared to exhaustive search. This approach could significantly reduce the computational cost of similarity search in large-scale embedding galleries, benefiting applications like face recognition, image retrieval, and recommendation systems where fast top-K retrieval is critical. The speedups are achieved for both original and reduced latent space dimensions, and dimensionality reduction further accelerates search while even improving rank-N metrics; however, the method requires finetuning the model, which may add training overhead and could be specific to ArcFace-based face recognition models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Top-K similarity search involves finding the K most similar items to a query in a large set of embeddings, which is typically done by exhaustive comparison and becomes costly as the gallery grows. Latent space configuration is a methodology that finetunes neural networks to produce embeddings with desired geometric properties, such as structured distributions that allow efficient pruning. ArcFace is a popular loss function for face recognition, and IResNet50 is a deep residual network architecture commonly used as its backbone.

## [Ground Truth Coverage Shapes Retrieval Scores in Clinical RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31606) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes an evidence-aware retrieval evaluation protocol for a clinical RAG system on breast lesion literature, showing that expanding ground-truth annotations from 25 to 37 evidence spans and from 36 to 51 accepted chunk targets increased recognized evidence without changing retrieval rankings. The best configuration used a hybrid retriever with 384-token chunks and 20% overlap, achieving Recall@3, R-Precision, MRR@3, and nDCG@3 of 0.94 each, with cross-encoder reranking yielding the highest scores. This work highlights that retrieval scores in clinical RAG evaluations are sensitive to annotation coverage, which can bias comparisons across chunking and retriever configurations. It matters for researchers and practitioners building evidence-based clinical RAG systems, as it underscores the need for careful ground-truth curation and independent validation. The study used a small corpus of 18 documents and 18 queries, evaluating chunk sizes from 128 to 1024 tokens, overlaps from 0 to 20%, and four retriever configurations. Generation was assessed on a 512-token index at K=1 to 5 using DeepEval claim-level metrics with deepseek-v4-pro as the LLM judge, and BLEU/ROUGE metrics; the authors note that the small development set and retrieval-informed curation require independent validation, including radiologist assessment of evidence sufficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 26, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems combine a retriever, which finds relevant documents, with a large language model that generates answers based on them. Retrieval quality is often measured with metrics like Recall@K, MRR, and nDCG@K, which compare retrieved results against ground-truth relevance annotations. In clinical domains, defining relevance is challenging because evidence may be spread across text chunks, and annotation coverage can affect evaluation outcomes.
